**By status:** [In the anthology](status/Active.md) (396) · [Watching](status/Proposed.md) (29) · [Unreviewed](status/Deferred.md) (181) · [Superseded](status/Superseded.md) (3) · [Attic](status/Rejected.md) (15)

What the status column means in this scheme — the words are luria's, the meanings are this project's.

| Status | | Means |
|---|---|---|
| `Active` | In the anthology | Worth reading, whether or not an account was drawn from it |
| `Proposed` | Watching | Promising and unproven; the note says what would settle it |
| `Deferred` | Unreviewed | In the corpus, not yet read closely enough to place |
| `Superseded` | Superseded | Later work covers this better, and the note names it |
| `Rejected` | Attic | Retired with a reason — too narrow, too theoretical, or simply not interesting enough to carry |

| # | Title | Summary | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [LIT-001](../../record/literature.d/LIT-001.md) v2 | Quantum Mechanics can be understood through stochastic optimization on spacetimes | Lindgren & Liukkonen (2019), DOI-10.1038/s41598-019-56357-3. The paper derives a Stueckelberg-type equation i∂φ/∂τ = (1/2m)□φ − Vφ (Eq. 36) from an HJB equation, but only after putting the imaginary unit in twice by hand. The first i is a factor √g = i multiplying the Lagrangian; standard relativity uses √(−g) = 1. The second is a complex "variance" σ² = i/m (Eq. 29), chosen so that the Hopf–Cole substitution J = log φ linearizes the equation. The linearization itself is the standard λ = σ²·(control cost) condition. The Schrödinger equation (Eq. 43) then comes from c → ∞ plus complex conjugation. The Dirac equation is not derived, and the "Klein–Gordon equation" (Eq. 40) has 2mV where m² should be. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-003](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-003.md)); the claim that the i comes from the Minkowski metric depends on the non-standard √g, not √(−g). The complex noise it also needs leaves no real stochastic process or optimization behind. Nelson (1966), Guerra–Morato (1983) and, for the control-theory side, Kappen (2005) cover the real content better. |
| [LIT-002](../../record/literature.d/LIT-002.md) v2 | Nested Hierarchical Dirichlet Processes | Paisley et al. (2012), [ARXIV-1210.6738](https://arxiv.org/abs/1210.6738). The nHDP gives each document its own DP over the children of every node of a shared global nCRP tree (G_i^(d) ~ DP(βG_i)), plus a per-node stop/continue switch U_{d,i} ~ Beta(γ1, γ2). Each word then takes its own path, and β → 0 recovers the single-path nCRP. The paper fits this with SVI plus a greedy per-document subtree selection. On small corpora the variational nHDP beats variational nCRP on all three datasets and Gibbs nCRP on two of three (PNAS −6.304 vs −6.496/−6.574 held-out log-likelihood). On 1.8M NYT and 2.7M Wikipedia documents it tops stochastic HDP and LDA-50/150/250 by roughly 0.08 and 0.11 nats (read off Figs. 2, 7), using ≈12 and ≈8 topics per document. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-023); the reference construction of the nHDP and a compact worked example of conjugate-exponential SVI for a tree-structured nonparametric model. Two things about it: the global update equations (23, 26–27) as printed contradict the paper's own §4.1.2 derivation, and the large-scale results never compare against the nCRP. |
| [LIT-003](../../record/literature.d/LIT-003.md) v2 | A structure theorem for generalized-noncontextual ontological models | Schmid et al. (2020), [ARXIV-2005.07161](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.07161). Take a tomographically local GPT and any convex-linear, empirically adequate, diagram-preserving map M from it into FVect_R. Then M(T) = χ_B ∘ T ∘ χ_A⁻¹ for unique invertible linear maps χ_A (Thm 4.1). Consequences: - Every diagram-preserving quasiprobabilistic model is an exact (non-overcomplete) frame representation (Prop. 4.3). - Every diagram-preserving noncontextual ontological model has \|Λ_A\| = dim(A), the GPT dimension (Cor. 4.2, §4.3): exactly d² ontic states for a qudit and 4 for a qubit. - The paper also proves (Cor. 3.5) that three things are equivalent in arbitrary compositional scenarios, without needing tomographic locality: a noncontextual ontological model of the operational theory; an ontological model (simplex embedding) of its GPT; and a positive quasiprobabilistic model of its GPT. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-017); It is the formal backbone of the "classicality = generalized noncontextuality = positive quasiprobability" position that LIT-007 and LIT-019 appeal to. The main theorem is fully proved and short. Read §3–4 and App. B. §5.2 illustrates why tomographic locality is necessary, and its numbers should be checked (see corrections). |
| [LIT-004](../../record/literature.d/LIT-004.md) v2 | Community Detection on Networks with Ricci Flow | Ni et al. (2019), [ARXIV-1907.03993](https://arxiv.org/abs/1907.03993). The paper runs Ollivier-Ricci flow w^{(k+1)}_{xy} = (1 − κ^{(k)}_{xy})·d^{(k)}(x,y) (= W(m_x, m_y)), then cuts edges above a weight threshold. On LFR (n = 500, avg. degree 20) this gives ARI ≈ 1 up to μ ≈ 0.6. On two-block SBM (n = 500, p_intra = 0.15) it gives ≈ 1 up to p_inter/p_intra = 0.5, but it collapses to ≈ 0.1 at 0.55, where Spinglass and Fast Greedy still score ≈ 0.9 and ≈ 0.8. Theorem 4.1 (α = p = 0, cliques G(a,b), a > b ≥ 2) proves in fact only that non-gateway intra-clique edges shrink as (1/a)ⁿ. Gateway intra-clique edges grow at the same rate λ₁ⁿ as inter-community edges, with ratio → k ∈ (0,1). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-006); the source paper for Ollivier-Ricci-flow community detection and the GraphRicciCurvature package, and worth reading for the method (§3, SI §C). It should be cited for the algorithm, not for superiority. Its SBM figure shows it losing to Spinglass and Fast Greedy past p_inter/p_intra = 0.5. Its theorem is weaker than stated, and it covers a parameter setting (α = p = 0) that differs from the one used in experiments (α = ½, p = 2). |
| [LIT-005](../../record/literature.d/LIT-005.md) v3 | Adding causality to the information-theoretic perspective on individuality | Bourrat (2024), DOI-10.1007/s13194-023-00566-1. Of Krakauer et al.'s (2020) three mutual-information "individuality" terms (A* = I(S_{t+1}; S_t), A = I(S_{t+1}; S_t \| E_t), nC = I(S_{t+1}; E_t \| S_t)), Bourrat argues that nC and A* are not individuality measures. He then proposes the interventional Â = I(S_{t+1}; Ŝ_t) (Eq. 6) and two ETI criteria: C1, a normalised Â_{S2}/H(Ŝ2) ≥ Â_{S1}/H(Ŝ1)\|θ₀, and C2, low Var(Â_{S2} \| Ê2 = ê2k). None of these is computed on any system. The paper's claim that Â ≡ I(S_{t+1}; S_t \| E_t) = A in Krakauer's model is false in general. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-027](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-027.md)); a clear conceptual argument that information-theoretic individuality should be interventional. It is unproven as a measure. Three things would settle it: a worked computation of Â, C1 and C2 on an explicit model (for example Black et al.'s ecological-scaffolding model); a corrected account of how Â relates to the observational A; and a correct adjustment set for its own Fig. 3 example. |
| [LIT-006](../../record/literature.d/LIT-006.md) v2 | Memorandum Opinion (remedies), United States v. Google LLC (D.D.C., No. 1:20-cv-03010-APM, ECF No. 1436) [descriptive title] | Mehta (2025), <https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.223205/gov.uscourts.dcd.223205.1436.0_1.pdf>. Remedies opinion after the 2024 §2 liability ruling. The court (Mehta, J.) bars exclusive distribution deals for Search, Chrome, Assistant and the Gemini app, with a one-year exit right on all payment deals. It does not ban payments, and it rejects the Chrome and Android divestitures and choice screens. It orders (i) a one-time snapshot of six Search Index fields per document, (ii) Glue and RankEmbed user-interaction training data "at least twice", capped and privacy-treated, and (iii) five-year search and text-ads syndication on ordinary commercial terms (search capped at 40% of queries in year one). "Qualified Competitors" is rewritten so it can include GenAI firms. The term is six years (five plus one of ramp-up). On GenAI, the court finds that grounded chatbots perform an information-retrieval function similar to general search engines, enough to bring GenAI inside the decree. It also finds that plaintiffs "did not establish that Google's scale advantage in Search translates into a quality advantage in GenAI search-assisted responses" (op. p. 156). For that reason it refuses to order sharing of Gemini training data. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-004](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-004.md)); a primary legal source, and the most detailed judicial record to date of how LLMs, grounding/RAG and the GenAI market were described under oath. It is worth reading for that and for the design of its data-sharing remedy. Its technical content is lay-level, built from testimony, and carries no instruction for ML practice. |
| [LIT-007](../../record/literature.d/LIT-007.md) v2 | Quasi-quantization: classical statistical theories with an epistemic restriction | Spekkens (2014), [ARXIV-1409.5041](https://arxiv.org/abs/1409.5041). Take a classical statistical theory over a phase space Ω = R^{2n} or (Z_d)^{2n}, and allow only uniform distributions over V^⊥ + v, where V is an isotropic subspace (a Poisson-commuting set of linear "quadrature" variables) and v ∈ V gives their values. The result is operationally identical to the quadrature quantum subtheory (the stabilizer formalism for odd prime d) for continuous variables and for odd prime d; the proof is that the Wigner functions of that subtheory are exactly these distributions (§IV.A). For d = 2 the two theories are isomorphic element by element but make different predictions, because the qubit stabilizer subtheory is contextual (§IV.B). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-013); the clearest single statement of the epistricted-theory programme, and the source of the weak/strong nonclassicality split that 2111.13727 and 2005.07161 build on; read it for §II–IV and App. A, not for Table II, which it asserts rather than derives. |
| [LIT-008](../../record/literature.d/LIT-008.md) v2 | Modeling and solving an integrated periodic vehicle routing and capacitated facility location problem in the context of solid waste collection | González et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2504.10648](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10648). The paper couples bin-capacity choice with weekly periodic capacitated routing (Sunday off, cyclic). Service time depends on the chosen bin combination, which gives an MIQP; a Glover linearisation turns it into a MILP. It solves both with Gurobi 10 for 8 h, and also with a binary-mask + permutation GA (CX 0.8, EM 0.05, pop 100, 10,000 generations). On five 12-point instances Gurobi stops at 26–32% optimality gaps. The best-of-30 GA overall cost is on average −0.08% vs the MILP incumbent and +1.70% vs the MIQP incumbent; the GA mean or median is +3.75% and +5.61%. The GA routes better (−5.75% and −5.74% routing cost) but buys much more bin capacity (+19.88% and +22.59%). On 40–163-point Bahía Blanca instances the GA returns feasible solutions in 0.72–5.58 h per run, with no bound to compare against. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-015); worth reading narrowly, for its formulation (Eqs. 1–2m, with the linearisation in 3a–5) and its public Bahía Blanca instances. The headline "GA matches exact solvers" should not be cited as a quality claim: the exact comparators are unproven incumbents with ~30% gaps, the GA was tuned on one of its five test instances, and the large instances have no reference at all. |
| [LIT-009](../../record/literature.d/LIT-009.md) v2 | An 800 Myr-old Impact Shower on the Terrestrial Planets from the Breakup of the Eulalia Parent Body | Bottke et al. (2026), [ARXIV-2606.05036](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05036). The paper builds a collisional-plus-dynamical source model. In its preferred Solution 5, the Eulalia parent body breaks up T = 865 Myr ago at a_c = 2.48 au next to the Jupiter 3:1 resonance. The model sends 6,995 D ≥ 2 km and 833 D ≥ 5 km fragments into the J3:1 (Table 1, before a ≈1.5× collisional correction). With impact probabilities per resonance entrant of 5.4×10⁻³ (Venus), 4.2×10⁻³ (Earth) and 0.6×10⁻³ (Mars), and the Moon at 1/20 of Earth, the expected largest impactor is D ≈ 8 km on Earth and Venus, 4.5 km on Mars and ">3 km" on the Moon. A Monte Carlo of 7 lunar projectiles puts the seven lunar craters dated 760–820 Ma "generally … within the 1σ" envelope. The authors conclude the breakup "can plausibly account for" the ~800 Ma lunar crater cluster, although on average the model's projectiles are too small to make Copernicus. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-002); a careful, well-sourced plausibility model for a hypothesised 800 Ma impact shower, and worth reading as the source model for it. It is unproven: its lunar dates are scaled from a single sample-dated crater (Copernicus), and only Solution 5, the most favourable, is carried forward. Radiometric dating of samples from the other six ~800 Ma craters (Al-Khwarizmi, Stefan, Saha, Godin, Das, Lowell), or a CI-like projectile signature at more than one of them, would settle it. The Earth, Mars and Venus material in §7 is self-declared speculation. |
| [LIT-010](../../record/literature.d/LIT-010.md) v2 | Quantum Thermodynamics | Vinjanampathy & Anders (2015), [ARXIV-1508.06099](https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.06099). A 2015–16 review with no new results, whose one synthesis (§5.2, §7) is that the field used four inequivalent notions of work: ensemble ⟨W⟩ = ∫tr[ρḢ]dt (Eq. 2), two-point-measurement W = E_m^(τ) − E_n^(0) (Eq. 24), ε-deterministic single-shot A^ε ≈ −kT ln(Z_Λ*/Z) (Eq. 49) and resource-theory w^max_ε = F^min_ε(ρ_S) − F(τ_S) (Eq. 50), of which only the last two coincide. It also judges that the field had "only made a small number of experimentally checkable predictions of new thermodynamic effects yet" (§7, p. 39). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-011); still worth reading as a short, accessible tour of where quantum thermodynamics stood in 2015–16, especially §3 (fluctuation theorems derived in full) and §5 (single-shot and resource-theory second laws). It is a map of the field, not an authority. It is dated: nothing after early 2016. Some formulas are garbled as printed (Eq. 68, the operator-sum statement, the Diesel strokes), so cite the primary papers it points to rather than the review. |
| [LIT-011](../../record/literature.d/LIT-011.md) v2 | Spectral Sets | Badea & Beckermann (2013), [ARXIV-1302.0546](https://arxiv.org/abs/1302.0546). A survey of when a set X ⊃ σ(A) satisfies ‖f(A)‖ ≤ K sup_X\|f\| for all rational f. Its headline entries are that the closure of the numerical range is completely K-spectral with a universal K_Crouzeix ∈ [2, 11.08] (Fact 107.7.2a; completely 2-spectral for 2×2, Fact 107.7.2b) and that σ(A) is K-spectral iff A is diagonalisable, with K the eigenvector condition number (Example 107.2.5). §107.8 turns these into Krylov error bounds such as Arnoldi ε_m ≤ 2K_Crouzeix ρ_{m−1}(f,E) for convex E ⊃ W(A). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-009); worth keeping as a compact, correctly referenced index of what was known in 2013 about bounding ‖f(A)‖ for non-normal A by sup-norms over the numerical range, pseudospectra, disks, lenses and annuli. Consult the relevant Fact rather than read it through. The universal numerical-range constant has since been improved: Crouzeix–Palencia (SIAM J. Matrix Anal. Appl., 2017) give 1+√2. That comes from outside the chapter and was not verified in this session. Check it before quoting 11.08 as current. |
| [LIT-012](../../record/literature.d/LIT-012.md) v2 | Transcript of Show Cause Hearing, Segundo A.P.G. v. Bondi et al. (D. Minn., No. 26-cv-603), Feb. 3, 2026 [descriptive title] | Blackwell (2026), <https://drive.google.com/file/d/1FnY2z7eb5efGlHrb2AYBtfqMVDJSUfIu/view>. At a 71-minute show-cause hearing on noncompliance with habeas release orders in five cases, Judge Blackwell said that the scale of Operation Metro Surge "is not a justification for diluting constitutional rights" and that the government "cannot … detain first and then sort out lawful authority later". The record includes a 13-day delay in releasing Oscar O.T., release conditions imposed without the court's authority on Jose L.C.C., and a Segundo release learned of only through another case. Government counsel attributed the failures to missing training, access and agency cooperation. The court took the matter under advisement. No written order following the hearing could be verified. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-014](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-014.md)); worth reading as a primary, certified record of a federal court questioning the executive about compliance with habeas release orders during Operation Metro Surge. It holds colloquy, not findings. Any holding would sit in a later written order, which could not be verified. |
| [LIT-013](../../record/literature.d/LIT-013.md) v2 | Nonparametric Bayesian Topic Modelling with the Hierarchical Pitman-Yor Processes | Lim et al. (2016), [ARXIV-1609.06783](https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.06783). The paper collapses every Pitman–Yor node to customer and table counts (Eq. 16, following Chen et al. 2011), which turns any HPYP topic model's posterior into a product of per-node factors, and it samples topics and counts jointly by decrement/propose/increment. The HPYP half of its Twitter-Network Topic Model uses this sampler; the network half is fit by a separate MH step. On one 240,517-tweet, 150-author corpus (T6) the TNTM reaches test perplexity 505.01 ± 7.8, against 664.25 ± 17.76 for a nonparametric ATM and 840.03 ± 15.7 for HDP-LDA, and network log-likelihood −500.63 against −557.86 for the random-function network model alone (Table 4). Removing the power-law discounts barely changes perplexity (508.64) but costs network likelihood (−560.28; Table 5). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-008); worth reading as a clear, self-contained tutorial on the table-count (Chen et al. 2011) collapsed blocked Gibbs sampler for hierarchical PYPs with discrete and mixture bases. The TNTM experiments are thin: one corpus, clustering baselines copied from another paper, and assertion-level claims about kernels and labels. Cite it for the sampler exposition, not for the empirical superiority of nonparametric models. |
| [LIT-014](../../record/literature.d/LIT-014.md) v3 | Reckless disregard for the truth coming from cops, doctors, and scientists: A rant. | Gelman (2025), <https://statmodeling.stat.columbia.edu/2025/06/24/reckless-disregard-for-the-truth-coming-from-cops-doctors-and-scientists-a-rant/>. Gelman argues that scientists (a 3-day study titled "long-term"; the power-pose abstract's "instantly become more powerful"), a paid medical expert (Ludmerer's 2002 "no opinion" on smoking and lung cancer) and DHS spokespeople (Lander "assaulting", Padilla "lunging", ICE's unsourced "413 percent" assaults) share one pattern, which he calls "reckless disregard for the truth". In his account it strengthens a weak case, is carried by journals, courts and media, is accepted as a term of art and goes unpunished. It is negative-sum, and bystanders who tolerate it sustain it. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-019](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-019.md)); worth reading as a short, clearly argued statement of an incentive account of socially accepted falsehood, with a relevant section on overclaiming in paper titles and abstracts. Its evidence is a handful of anecdotes, and its police examples rest on press accounts that this reading did not check. |
| [LIT-015](../../record/literature.d/LIT-015.md) v2 | Joint Publication 3-12, Cyberspace Operations | Joint Chiefs of Staff (2018), <https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/document/16681-joint-chiefs-staff-joint-publication-3-12>. JP 3-12 (8 June 2018) defines cyberspace operations as "the employment of cyberspace capabilities where the primary purpose is to achieve objectives in or through cyberspace" (p. I-1). It sorts every non-"cyberspace-enabled" military action in cyberspace into exactly three missions: DODIN operations, DCO (split into DCO-IDM and DCO-RA) and OCO. Missions are categorized "based only on the intent or objective of the issuing authority" (p. II-2). Missions are carried out through four actions: cyberspace security, defense, exploitation and attack. On authorities, the publication says only that authority "is derived from the US Constitution and federal law" (Titles 10, 50 and 32 named as key; p. III-2), plus SecDef policy and President/SecDef orders. Specific authorities are deferred to the unreleased classified Appendix A. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-025](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-025.md)); the latest public edition of joint cyberspace doctrine and the primary source for its definitions and mission taxonomy. A December 2022 revision is reported (in secondary sources only, not seen here) to exist with distribution limited to CAC holders; if it is ever filed, this becomes Superseded by it. |
| [LIT-016](../../record/literature.d/LIT-016.md) v2 | The sheaf-theoretic structure of non-locality and contextuality | Abramsky & Brandenburger (2011), [ARXIV-1102.0264](https://arxiv.org/abs/1102.0264). For finite measurement scenarios, an empirical model has a factorizable (local / non-contextual) hidden-variable realization iff its compatible family of distributions extends to a global section (Thm 8.1). Over the reals every no-signalling model has such a section (Thm 5.9: "negative-probability LHV" ⇔ no-signalling, because both span the same subspace, of dimension D = Σ_{U∈𝒰}(l−1)^{\|U\|}, Thm 5.4). Contextuality comes in three strictly nested strengths (probabilistic ⊋ possibilistic ⊋ strong), with the Bell, Hardy and GHZ(n≥3) models as witnesses of each level. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-016); the founding paper of the sheaf-theoretic approach to contextuality. It is short, fully proved and still the standard reference for its definitions (empirical model, strong contextuality, the global-section criterion). Later work (cohomology, the contextual fraction, the CbD comparison) extends it but does not replace it. |
| [LIT-017](../../record/literature.d/LIT-017.md) v2 | Spectral Networks and Betti Lagrangians | Casals & Nho (2025), [ARXIV-2504.08144](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08144). For any rank n, and for Lagrangians with non-algebraic (positive-braid) asymptotics, spectral networks exist and are characterised by pseudo-holomorphic strips in the adiabatic limit (Theorems 1–2). For exact Betti Lagrangians, a Family Floer functor equals Gaiotto–Moore–Neitzke non-abelianization in the adiabatic limit (Theorem 3, i.e. 5.24) and is realised by A∞-operations in wrapped and partially wrapped Fukaya categories of T*S (Theorem 4). The analysis in §§4–5 leans on Ekholm 2007 and on the second author's 2024 preprint, and Theorem 5 (spectral networks from Demazure weaves) is recorded without a written proof. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-022); an unrefereed v1 preprint that is carefully motivated, but whose analytic core (§4 adiabatic convergence, §5 continuation strips) leans on Ekholm 2007 and on the second author's 2024 preprint, and whose Theorem 5 has no written proof. What would settle it: a refereed version or independent expert check of §§4–5, a written proof of Proposition 7.5, and theorem statements narrowed to the hypotheses the proofs use (creative networks, exactness, trimmed limits). Only §1 is useful to a non-specialist here. |
| [LIT-018](../../record/literature.d/LIT-018.md) v3 | Distinguishability and Accessible Information in Quantum Theory | Fuchs (1995), [arXiv:quant-ph/9601020](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/9601020). Minimizing the Bhattacharyya overlap Σ_b √(tr ρ0E_b)√(tr ρ1E_b) over all POVMs gives exactly F(ρ0,ρ1) = tr√(ρ1^{1/2}ρ0ρ1^{1/2}) (Eq. 3.28). The minimum is attained by a projective measurement in the eigenbasis of M = ρ1^{-1/2}√(ρ1^{1/2}ρ0ρ1^{1/2})ρ1^{-1/2} (Eq. 3.67). For binary ensembles, the same Schwarz-inequality technique gives three results: a short proof of the Holevo bound I(t) ≤ S(t), with equality iff [ρ0,ρ1] = 0 (Eq. 3.149); a tighter upper bound L(t) (Eq. 3.187, integrated in Eqs. 3.230/3.244; closed form for qubits in Eq. 3.269); and a measurement-attained lower bound M(t) = tr[(1−t)ρ0 ln L_ρ(ρ0) + tρ1 ln L_ρ(ρ1)] (Eq. 3.203). The thesis also proves that two states can be broadcast iff they commute, and cloned iff they are identical or orthogonal (§4.3, proof written for invertible ρ0, ρ1). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-021); still the most self-contained derivation of mixed-state fidelity as an optimal classical overlap, and of the Holevo bound by the curvature (J'' vs S'') argument. Read §3.3 and §3.5.1–3.5.2 for those, and Ch. 2 as a tutorial on why error probability, the Chernoff bound, KL and mutual information are the operationally grounded classical measures. The quantum-Kullback bounds (§3.6), the inference–disturbance formalism (§4.2) and the no-broadcasting proof (§4.3) are preliminary or appeared in companion papers. Read those papers for their final forms. |
| [LIT-019](../../record/literature.d/LIT-019.md) v2 | Why interference phenomena do not capture the essence of quantum theory | Catani et al. (2021), [ARXIV-2111.13727](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.13727). The paper models each mode of the interferometer as a classical bit pair (occupation N ∈ Z_2, phase Φ ∈ Z_2). The agent may know only one of N, Φ or N⊕Φ and must be maximally ignorant of the rest. Under that restriction, local and deterministic dynamics (beamsplitter: swap N_L with Φ_L⊕Φ_R) reproduce exactly the 50-50, ϕ ∈ {0, π} Mach–Zehnder statistics (output port L with probability 1 or 0; ½/½ with a which-way detector). The same holds for the Elitzur–Vaidman bomb tester (success probability ¼), delayed choice and the quantum eraser (interference within each P_A outcome class, none within each Q_A class). So this "TRAP" slice of interference phenomenology cannot, on its own, force wave–particle complementarity, observer-dependence or nonlocal/retrocausal influence. The paper leaves aside the phenomenology it concedes is nonclassical (unbalanced beamsplitters, the functional form of the duality relation, Hardy's interferometer). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-018); It is the worked, experiment-by-experiment defence of placing interference in the weakly nonclassical column of LIT-007's Table II, and it has the clearest statement of the Spekkens-school methodology (define classicality, formalize it, prove a no-go). Read §3, §4.3, §5.1 and App. A. Its noncontextuality claim for the toy field theory is asserted, not proved. |
| [LIT-020](../../record/literature.d/LIT-020.md) v2 | Characterizing Complex Networks with Forman-Ricci Curvature and Associated Geometric Flows | Weber et al. (2016), [ARXIV-1607.08654](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.08654). The paper specializes Forman's (2003) Bochner–Weitzenböck curvature on weighted CW complexes to graphs, giving the closed-form edge curvature Ric_F(e) = ω(e)[ω(v1)/ω(e) + ω(v2)/ω(e) − Σ_{e_v1∼e, e_v2∼e}(ω(v1)/√(ω(e)ω(e_v1)) + ω(v2)/√(ω(e)ω(e_v2)))] (Eq. 11). It then proposes three uses: an EMD distance between curvature distributions, which puts Facebook, Google and BioGrid graphs at 0.050–0.060 from a Barabási–Albert model against 0.17–0.36 from Erdős–Rényi and Watts–Strogatz (Table 1); a discrete "2-D-form" Ricci flow γ̃(e) − γ(e) = −Ric_F(γ(e))·γ(e) (Eq. 36) for change detection; and a Laplacian flow ∂G/∂t = (□₁ − F)G (Eq. 45) for denoising. The authors say the flow applications are "only theoretically proposed and illustrated solely on a small example" (§5). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-026); the standard early citation for Forman–Ricci curvature *on networks* and for the Forman–Ricci flow. Read it for §2.3 (Eqs. 11–17) and §4.1 (Eqs. 36–41), and cite it for definitions only. Its empirical and application claims are one-table or one-figure demonstrations. Several of its formulas contain sign and consistency slips, listed below, so a user should take Eq. 11 from here and check it against Sreejith et al. 2016. |
| [LIT-021](../../record/literature.d/LIT-021.md) v3 | From the origin of life to pandemics: emergent phenomena in complex systems | Artime & De Domenico (2022), [ARXIV-2205.11595](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.11595). An editorial review with no new result. Its one formal item (§4, Eq. 4.1: x(t+1) = f[x(t)], x(0) = x₀) sorts phenomena three ways. Non-emergent: local rules plus initial conditions let you deduce the "expected" high-level pattern. Weakly emergent: they let you deduce an "unexpected" one by computation. Strongly emergent: no deduction is possible even in principle. "Unexpected" is never defined. The rest is a cross-domain survey (§3), a 16-paper map of the theme issue (§5), and an outlook (§6). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-007); worth reading as the table of contents and framing of the Phil. Trans. A theme issue that holds LIT-025 and LIT-027 (§5), and as a quick survey of emergence examples. Do not cite it for a definition of emergence, because its "mathematical" definition leaves the deciding criterion ("unexpected") informal. |
| [LIT-022](../../record/literature.d/LIT-022.md) v2 | Is the United States Still a Liberal Democracy? | Drezner (2025), <https://danieldrezner.substack.com/p/is-the-united-states-still-a-liberal>. Drezner asks whether the US will still be a liberal constitutional democracy in January 2029. He reviews Polity's downgrades (+10 in 2015 to +5 for 2020, and in February 2025 "at the cusp of autocracy"), the Freedom House fall from 92 to 83 (2014–2021, as quoted from Levitsky & Way) and counter-evidence (continued compliance with court orders; Hickman's "not there yet"). He then concludes that "it is hard to disagree" with Levitsky & Way's forecast of competitive authoritarianism, and expects democracy's "precarity" to be the lasting condition. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-001](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-001.md)); a short, fair synthesis of the 2025 democracy-index downgrades, roughly half of it quotation; its central forecast is Levitsky & Way's ("The Path to American Authoritarianism", Foreign Affairs, Feb 2025), which is the primary source to read and is not held here. |
| [LIT-023](../../record/literature.d/LIT-023.md) v2 | Russian Hybrid Warfare | Clark (2020), <https://www.understandingwar.org/sites/default/files/Russian%20Hybrid%20Warfare%20ISW%20Report%202020.pdf>. Clark argues from open Russian military writing of 2015–2020, chiefly Military Thought and Army Collection, that Russia treats "hybrid war" as a type of war. In his account it is a strategic-level effort to reorient a target state, in which every action, conventional force included, is subordinate to an information campaign. He concludes that a US strategy centred on deterring great-power conventional war can "technically succeed" while losing. The report ends with 12 recommendations (executive-summary count). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-012](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-012.md)); worth reading as a clear, openly sourced statement of the "hybrid war is a type of war, not a toolkit" reading of 2015–2020 Russian military journals. Its representativeness rests on argument, not on a systematic sample, and its forward-looking claims predate 2022 and are not tested here. |
| [LIT-024](../../record/literature.d/LIT-024.md) v2 | Memorandum and Order granting motions to quash, In re Administrative Subpoena 25-1431-032 to Rhode Island Hospital (D.R.I., No. 1:26-mc-00007-MSM-AEM, ECF No. 38) [descriptive title] | McElroy (2026), <https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.rid.62049/gov.uscourts.rid.62049.38.0.pdf>. Judge McElroy quashed DOJ HIPAA administrative subpoena No. 25-1431-032 (18 U.S.C. § 3486) to Rhode Island Hospital on three independent grounds: 1. no congressionally authorized purpose, because DOJ's FDCA off-label "misbranding" theory against a prescribing hospital is not legally cognizable (Facteau; 21 U.S.C. §§ 396, 353(b)(2)); 2. issuance for an improper purpose in bad faith; 3. a Fourteenth Amendment informational-privacy violation for the children, with relief extended to all minors because a DCYF-only carve-out would itself expose DCYF wards. The court enjoined DOJ with respect to patient-identifying information and PHI produced under the subpoena, and stated that it quashed the subpoena, "not the enforcement order entered by the Texas court". An amended order the next day (ECF 44) added "seeking" to the injunction. DOJ appealed (1st Cir. No. 26-1568; argument calendared for Oct 7, 2026). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-024](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-024.md)); A primary source worth reading for the informational-privacy holding and the inter-circuit procedural conflict. It is not final: it is on appeal, a First Circuit motions panel (concurrence) has questioned its merits, and it relies in part on QueerDoc, which the Ninth Circuit has since reversed. Read it with ECF 44, the May 18 Texas order and the May 19 First Circuit order. |
| [LIT-025](../../record/literature.d/LIT-025.md) v4 | Greater than the parts: a review of the information decomposition approach to causal emergence | Mediano et al. (2022), [ARXIV-2111.06518](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.06518). A review, with no new theorem, of the ΦID theory of causal emergence. A supervenient feature V_t is k-th-order causally emergent iff Un⁽ᵏ⁾(V_t; X_t′ \| X_t) > 0. A system hosts such features iff Syn⁽ᵏ⁾(X_t; X_t′) > 0 (cited as Rosas et al. 2020, Theorem 1), and Syn⁽ᵏ⁾ = D⁽ᵏ⁾ + G⁽ᵏ⁾ (downward causation plus causal decoupling). The review's own additions are interpretive: the formalism is neutral between Granger and Pearl, coordinate dependence is defended as a feature, and it rebuts the charge that the theory makes no macro-versus-micro comparison. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-010); a short, accurate map of the ΦID emergence formalism and the right entry point to Rosas et al. 2020, which a reader will still need for the proofs, the assumptions and the redundancy-free practical criteria. None of these is stated in the review. |
| [LIT-026](../../record/literature.d/LIT-026.md) | Comparative proteomic analysis of two wasps venom, Vespa tropica and Vespa affinis | Rungsa et al. (2016), DOI-10.1016/j.toxicon.2016.06.005. Venom from the great banded wasp (V. tropica) is more potent than venom from the banded tiger wasp (V. affinis): PD50 of 3 versus 12.5 µg/g. The authors attribute the difference to higher levels and activity of hyaluronidase (about 2.5× the proportion), phospholipase and dipeptidyl peptidase, since the two venoms' protein profiles are otherwise similar. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-25; not read in full |
| [LIT-027](../../record/literature.d/LIT-027.md) v4 | Emergence as the conversion of information: a unifying theory | Varley & Hoel (2022), [ARXIV-2104.13368](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.13368). Varley and Hoel apply a PID, with the Williams–Beer I_min redundancy, to past→future mutual information and summarise it with a new layer-weighted "synergy bias" B_syn ∈ [0,1]. Coarse-graining can raise B_syn at the macroscale. A macroscale XOR reaches 0.833, against 0.52 for its NAND/AND/OR implementation. In 200 random 3-node "Gaussian" TPMs expanded into equivalence-class micro/mesoscales with *identical* MI, every macroscale had higher B_syn, by ≈ 0.01–0.07 (r = 0.819 against macroscale B_syn). In 185 near-deterministic systems the change was mostly negative, down to ≈ −0.1. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-020); a clear, small-scale demonstration that coarse-graining can change the redundant/synergistic composition of information and not just its amount. It is worth reading as the explicit cross-scale counterpoint to the ΦID account (item 21). Its "unifying theory" is a framing: the EI section re-describes a known decomposition and contains no new result. |
| [LIT-028](../../record/literature.d/LIT-028.md) v2 | Asymptotic versus mesoscopic spectral dimensions in networks and inhomogeneous structures | Grimaldi et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2510.20520](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20520). The paper studies a "bundled" network, meaning a fiber graph attached at one node to every node of a base graph, with base and fiber growing with the same length L. For such a network, first-order perturbation theory (SM Eq. 10) gives the low spectrum as λ = ℓ^b/N_f, and from that the gap ("Fiedler") dimension d_g = 2(d_f,f + d_f,b)/(d_f,f + 2d_f,b/d_g,b), while the spectral dimension stays d_s = d_s,f. The two therefore differ unless d_g,b = 2 or the fiber is finite. For example, the ring-of-rings comb has d_s = 1 but d_g = 4/3, and the 2D-base brush has d_s = 1 but d_g = 2. The rule holds only while the base-induced gap lies below the fiber's own gap (SM Eq. 14). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-005); the base-controlled gap rule and its derivation are correct and easy to check, and they are worth reading as a clean, minimal example of the density-of-states exponent and the gap exponent coming apart. Read the SM finite-size scaling forms (Eqs. 25, 29) with care: their N prefactors are wrong except when d_s,b = d_g. |
| [LIT-029](../../record/literature.d/LIT-029.md) | Long-Term Cognitive and Neuropsychiatric Consequences of Repetitive Concussion and Head-Impact Exposure | McAllister & McCrea (2017), DOI-10.4085/1062-6050-52.1.14. This review concludes that most athletes' symptoms recover within about 1–2 weeks of a concussion. Evidence that repetitive concussion or sub-concussive head impacts cause long-term cognitive decline, neuropsychiatric change or neurodegeneration (including CTE) is mixed and preliminary, with signals of vulnerability in subgroups (e.g., verbal memory). Settling it needs large prospective longitudinal studies with baselines and controls. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-25; not read in full |
| [LIT-030](../../record/literature.d/LIT-030.md) v2 | A Counterexample to the Mizohata-Takeuchi Conjecture | Cairo (2025), [ARXIV-2502.06137](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06137). For every C² hypersurface Σ ⊂ ℝᵈ not lying in a hyperplane (implicitly: one with a point of non-zero curvature; see corrections), and every large R, there are f ∈ L²(Σ) and a weight w = \|ĥ\|² with supp ĥ ⊂ B_{CR} such that ∫\|Ef\|²w ≳ log R · ‖f‖²₂ · sup_ℓ ∫_ℓ w (Thm 1.2 via Prop. 3.1). So the Mizohata–Takeuchi conjecture and Stein's conjecture (Conj. 1.4) are false as stated, and the CHV route to loss-free endpoint multilinear restriction is closed. The mechanism is a lattice Q of middle-layer subset sums of N ≍ log R points on Σ, chosen so that no hyperplane meets more than 2^{d−1} of the R⁻¹-balls around Q (Lemma 3.5). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-051); a short, readable disproof of a 40-year-old conjecture in Fourier restriction. The counting argument is checkable by a non-specialist; the incidence lemma (§4) is the part that needs an expert. It is worth reading as mathematics. The "local" replacement it proposes (Conj. 1.5) has since been refuted for a dense family of C^k hypersurfaces by Cairo–Zhang (arXiv:2512.08064), so it should be read together with that paper. |
| [LIT-031](../../record/literature.d/LIT-031.md) v2 | Moving away from lexicalism in psycho- and neuro-linguistics | Krauska & Lau (2023), DOI-10.3389/flang.2023.1125127. The authors argue that lemma-based production models encode two lexicalist assumptions: a morphology/syntax split, and a stored meaning–syntax–form "triad". On that basis the models mishandle Inuktitut polysynthesis, separable Vietnamese idiomatic collocations, Hiaki number suppletion and English verb–object idioms. Updating the representations (treelets) is not enough, because the algorithms — separate lexical retrieval and structure building, word-sized increments — also have to change. They sketch an 8-stage non-lexicalist model and derive qualitative aphasia predictions from it. There are no new data and no implementation. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-029](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-029.md)); This is a clear diagnosis of where lemma models build in lexicalism, but its positive model is a verbal sketch (the full model is "in prep.") and its aphasia predictions are untested; an implemented model, or lesion or aphasia data that separate its predictions from lemma-model ones, would settle it. |
| [LIT-032](../../record/literature.d/LIT-032.md) v2 | A type theory for synthetic ∞-categories | Riehl & Shulman (2017), [ARXIV-1705.07442](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.07442). A three-layer type theory adds a directed interval 2 to HoTT. Its layers are cubes, topes (the coherent theory of a strict interval) and types, and simplices become shapes that can be mapped out of by extension types. One contractibility condition, "Segal" (unique fillers for the 2-1-horn), then yields associativity (Prop. 5.9) and 3-horn fillers (Prop. 5.21). It also makes every function a functor (Prop. 6.1), gives a dependent Yoneda lemma for covariant families (Thm. 9.5), and makes having a left adjoint a proposition when the adjoint's codomain is Rezk (Thm. 11.23). The bisimplicial-sets semantics is sketched. Segal and Rezk types are shown to match Segal and complete Segal spaces (Props. A.21, A.25), and soundness is not proved in full. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-069); This is the founding paper of simplicial HoTT, where synthetic (∞,1)-category theory starts. It is worth reading as mathematics by anyone following directed type theory. Its semantics appendix is explicitly a sketch. |
| [LIT-033](../../record/literature.d/LIT-033.md) v2 | Disenshittify or die! How hackers can seize the means of computation and build a new, good internet that is hardened against our asshole bosses' insatiable horniness for enshittification [DEF CON 32 talk] | Doctorow (2024), <https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4EmstuO0Em8>. Doctorow argues that platforms follow a three-stage decay cycle: good to users while locking them in, then good to business customers at users' expense, then taking all the value for themselves. The mechanism is "twiddling". It became possible when four disciplining forces weakened: competition, regulation, interoperability and tech-worker power. A "new, good internet" needs all four restored, with interop (reverse-engineering rights) and unionisation as the hacker-specific levers. The argument is assertion and anecdote throughout. It carries several checkable factual slips, notably about EU law and the court in United States v. Google. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-041](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-041.md)); The most compact first-person statement of the "four forces" version of the enshittification thesis, worth reading as advocacy with its evidence taken on trust. Its figures and legal claims need their own sources before anything rests on them. |
| [LIT-034](../../record/literature.d/LIT-034.md) v2 | The sound of an axon's growth | Folz et al. (2018), [ARXIV-1807.04799](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.04799). The paper proposes that an axon can set its length from motor-generated oscillations without reading their frequency. Two Hill-type stages turn the period (which grows with length L) into a shape change and then into a change in the mean of a contractility signal R. Coupling growth-cone protrusion (v_g c_I) against R-driven contraction (v_s c_R) then gives a stable length: about 125 µm with sub-µm (< 1 %) oscillation for the Fig. 4a parameters. In part of parameter space the final length rises as kinesin influx J0 falls, or as dynein coupling J_O falls, qualitatively as in experiments. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-067); A short, self-contained theory letter worth reading for its frequency-to-amplitude transduction argument (Eqs. 1–4) and the delay-equation length controller. It is a proof of mechanism, not a model fitted to axons: it has one parameter set, it never states the motor velocity v that converts its lengths into micrometres, and it proposes no experimental discriminator from gradient mechanisms. |
| [LIT-035](../../record/literature.d/LIT-035.md) v2 | The ergodicity solution of the cooperation puzzle | Peters & Adamou (2022), DOI-10.1098/rsta.2020.0425. If N entities' resources follow GBM (drift μ, volatility σ, independent noise), then pooling and sharing equally every step leaves the drift at μ, cuts the volatility to σ/√N (Eq. 3.9), and raises each member's time-average growth rate from μ − σ²/2 to μ − σ²/(2N) (Eq. 4.1). The premium is (σ²/2)(1 − 1/N) (Eq. 4.2). With pairwise correlation ρ the effective volatility is σ√((1 + (N−1)ρ)/N), and the large-N limit is μ − σ²ρ/2 (Eqs. 5.12–5.13). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-059](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-059.md)); worth reading as the clearest short derivation of the risk-pooling (geometric-mean) advantage of sharing under multiplicative noise. The mathematics is correct and self-contained. The framing claim, that this "solves" the cooperation puzzle and makes cooperation the default, rests on the model's zero-cost, no-cheating assumptions, and the paper's own citations (McCloskey, Winterhalder, Uitdehaag, Kennedy et al.) show the core result was already known. |
| [LIT-036](../../record/literature.d/LIT-036.md) v2 | Self-organization of primitive metabolic cycles due to non-reciprocal interactions | Ouazan-Reboul et al. (2023), [ARXIV-2303.09832](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.09832). For M chemotactic catalyst species in a closed cycle, where species m converts chemical m into chemical m+1, the linearised long-wavelength interaction matrix is circulant, so its eigenvalues are Re λ_ℓ = −(αρ0/D)(μ(p)−μ(s))[1−cos(2πℓ/M)] and Im λ_ℓ = (αρ0/D)(μ(s)+μ(p)) sin(2πℓ/M) (Eq. 5). The homogeneous state is unstable exactly when the species are self-attracting, μ(p) − μ(s) < 0 (Eq. 6). For even M the leading mode is real, with stoichiometry (1,−1,1,…), so same-parity species co-aggregate. For odd M the leading mode is a complex pair, and with chasing interactions this gives persistent "invasion–explosion" oscillations. The nonlinear phases were checked by Brownian dynamics at M = 5 and M = 6 only. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-079); Worth reading as a clean, fully analytic example of how cyclic non-reciprocal coupling produces a circulant spectrum and an odd/even dichotomy. The origin-of-life framing is a proposal the paper does not test, and the authors concede in the peer-review file that the chemotactic mobilities of real prebiotic catalysts are unknown. |
| [LIT-037](../../record/literature.d/LIT-037.md) v3 | The Open Systems View | Cuffaro & Hartmann (2021), [ARXIV-2112.11095](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.11095). The paper argues philosophically, not formally, that the quantum theory of open systems "GT" is more fundamental than standard closed-system quantum theory "ST". GT treats states as density operators that evolve, in general, non-unitarily, and it admits not-completely-positive maps. The ontic and epistemic cases each win only on a second, interpretation- or practice-laden criterion: OntFund-2 and EpFund-2. On the first, formal criteria (OntFund-1, EpFund-1) the two frameworks tie, and the authors say so because Stinespring dilation and dropping the Lindblad terms translate in both directions (§4.1, §4.2.1). Explanatory fundamentality is granted only deflationarily (ExFund-1), and the authors recommend dropping the notion (§4.2.2). The step from there to "the open systems view is fundamental tout court" (§4.3) rests on the special sciences and on "there are no other options". | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-077); worth reading as the clearest statement of the "open systems view" and of the distinction between a view, a framework, a theory and a model (§2, §4.1, §5.2). Read it knowing that its fundamentality verdict is carried by interpretive premises (OntFund-2, EpFund-2) and a deflationary step, not by any formal asymmetry between the frameworks. |
| [LIT-038](../../record/literature.d/LIT-038.md) v2 | The Relativity of Causal Knowledge | D'Acunto & Battiloro (2025), [ARXIV-2503.11718](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11718). The paper defines SCMs as functors I → Prob and interventions as natural transformations (which requires invertible mixing functions). It encodes each non-intervened SCM as the convex space Δ(V, Ω) of all probability measures on its endogenous space, and puts these spaces on a graph as stalks of a network sheaf and cosheaf. The restriction maps are interventionally consistent α-abstractions and the extension maps are unspecified affine maps. "Relative causal knowledge" is the image of alternating restriction and extension along a path (Def. 11). The two substantive-looking theorems are either unsound or vacuous. Theorem 1 (every convex combination of a model's interventional measures is a soft-interventional measure of it) rests on a proof step that is false. Theorems 2 and 3 hold for any pushforward map, so interventional consistency does no work in them. There are no results about the sheaf. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-050); Not worth a reader's time as a source of results. Its one non-trivial theorem is unsound as proved and false in general, its encoding theorem is vacuous, and §4 contains definitions only. A reader wanting category-theoretic causal abstraction should go to Rischel's thesis or Beckers–Halpern. A reader wanting sheaves on networks should go to Curry or Hansen–Ghrist. |
| [LIT-039](../../record/literature.d/LIT-039.md) | Rigorous renormalization group | Kupiainen (2023), DOI-10.1038/s41567-023-02233-x. In this Comment, Kupiainen summarizes efforts to put renormalization-group calculations on a mathematically rigorous footing, starting from RG's origin as an ad hoc way of handling divergences in perturbation theory. The standfirst supports only that framing; the specific results covered can only be inferred from the reference list. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-25; not read in full |
| [LIT-040](../../record/literature.d/LIT-040.md) v2 | two-level type theory [descriptive title: nLab wiki entry "two-level type theory"] | nLab (2018), <https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/two-level+type+theory>. Two-level type theory pairs a homotopy (univalent, fibrant) type theory with an outer level having UIP "exact equality" (the internalised metatheory). The nLab page is an accurate but thin gloss on two-level type theory. It says nothing about the result that makes 2LTT usable: basic 2LTT is **conservative over HoTT** (ACKS Prop. 2.18: an inner type provably inhabited in 2LTT is inhabited in HoTT), which answers much of the page's own "open question" about the principle of equivalence. It also omits that a fibrant-replacement type former would force UIP on the inner level (ACKS Thm 2.20). The paper it cites, ACKS arXiv:1705.03307, is the proper source: definitions via models, conservativity, the axiom menu (A1)–(A6)/(T1)–(T3), and internal Reedy-fibrant diagrams over inverse categories with a definition of univalent (∞,1)-category. | Superseded — by [LIT-095](../../record/literature.d/LIT-095.md); read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-047](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-047.md)); superseded by Annenkov–Capriotti–Kraus–Sattler, "Two-Level Type Theory and Applications" (arXiv:1705.03307; MSCS 2023, DOI 10.1017/S0960129523000130), which the page itself cites and which covers everything the page says, with proofs and the conservativity theorem the page lacks. That paper is now filed as LIT-095, and this page is superseded by it. |
| [LIT-041](../../record/literature.d/LIT-041.md) v2 | The Work Capacity of Channels with Memory: Maximum Extractable Work in Percept-Action Loops | Fiderer et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2504.06209](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06209). The paper models agent and environment as coupled finite hidden-Markov channels. It defines the environment's work capacity C_work = max over agent models of ⟨H(A_t\|M_t) − H(S_t\|M_t)⟩_t (in k_BT ln 2 per round) and proves two things. First, for unifilar environments whose percepts ignore actions, the efficient agents are exactly the randomize-and-forget ∩ maximally-predictive ones, and C_work = log\|A\| − h(S). Second, one binary memoryless environment exists where predictive, max-entropy-action and efficient agents form three nonempty, pairwise-disjoint sets: every predictive agent extracts ≤ 0, uniform-random agents extract 1 − h(3/4) ≈ 0.189 bits, and the optimum is ½·log₂(3/4 + 1/√2) ≈ 0.272 bits, reached by a memoryless agent that plays action 0 with probability 1/√2. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-032); worth reading for anyone working on the thermodynamics of prediction or agency. It gives a clean, fully proved extension of the tape-setting results (Boyd–Mandal–Crutchfield 2018) to feedback loops, and a sharp counterexample to "prediction is necessary for efficiency". Read it knowing that the headline "trade-off" is shown by one existence example, that the example's optimum simply forgets everything, and that Table I prints the memoryless-channel capacity with its sign flipped. |
| [LIT-042](../../record/literature.d/LIT-042.md) v2 | Shannon invariants: A scalable approach to information decomposition | Gutknecht et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2504.15779](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15779). For any set of quantities Π(α) satisfying the PID consistency equation (A7), whatever the redundancy measure, two averages are fixed by Shannon quantities alone. They are the average degree of redundancy, r̄ = Σᵢ I(Xᵢ;Y)/I(X;Y) (Prop. 1), and the average degree of vulnerability, v̄ = Σⱼ I(Xⱼ;Y\|X₋ⱼ)/I(X;Y) (Prop. 2). Two identities follow: RSI = (r̄ − 1)·I(X;Y) (Cor. 1), and a new "dual" index DRSI = I(X;Y) − Σⱼ I(Xⱼ;Y\|X₋ⱼ) = (1 − v̄)·I(X;Y) (Prop. 4, Cor. 2). In small, stochastically quantised (8-level) networks analysed on their own training set, r̄ about the label rises with depth and v̄ falls with depth and training. In a face autoencoder, decoder layers are more redundant than size-matched encoder layers, and r̄ rises with bottleneck width. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-045); the theory (§II–III, App. B) is short, correct and redundancy-measure-free. It gives the right reading of the old redundancy–synergy index and is worth reading for that. The "scalable" deep-learning half is a demonstration on tiny, quantised networks with information computed on the training set as the population. Read it as a proof of concept, not as evidence about realistic models. |
| [LIT-043](../../record/literature.d/LIT-043.md) | This Secret Math Equation let the US Government Spy on Anyone | Kibicho (2025), <https://leetarxiv.substack.com/p/dual-ec-backdoor-coding-gui…>. The post is a paywalled programming tutorial that implements the Dual_EC_DRBG random-number-generator backdoor in Python, as a chapter of a book on finite fields for programmers. Nothing beyond that framing was reachable. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-25; not read in full |
| [LIT-044](../../record/literature.d/LIT-044.md) v2 | The Epic Story of Maximum Likelihood | Stigler (2007), [ARXIV-0804.2996](https://arxiv.org/abs/0804.2996). Stigler gives a history of maximum likelihood from Lagrange (1769) to Le Cam (1953). Fisher gave three optimality arguments. The 1922 one rests on the false belief that ML estimates are always sufficient. The 1925 "ANOVA" one derives the information inequality V ≥ 1/I(θ) from Var X = E Var(X\|T) + Var E(X\|T), treating approximate normality as exact. The third, sent privately to Hotelling in 1930, holds for multinomial models and smooth degree-0-homogeneous estimators, and was published only in disguise in 1935. Stigler's verdict is that the Neyman–Scott growing-parameter example (Wald 1938, published 1948) is the practically important failure. He calls Hodges's 1951 superefficiency example "an ingenious but minor technical achievement", because the superefficiency set has Lebesgue measure zero (Le Cam 1953). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-074); an authoritative, archive-based history by the leading historian of statistics, and worth reading for exactly which ML optimality claims were proved, under what conditions, and which counterexamples matter. It is a history, not a technical reference: for modern conditions it points to Bahadur (1964), Le Cam (1953, 1990), van der Vaart (1998) and Cox (2006). |
| [LIT-045](../../record/literature.d/LIT-045.md) v5 | Structural Realism | Ladyman (2007), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/structural-realism/>. The entry presents structural realism (Worrall 1989) as the attempt to keep both the no-miracles argument and the lesson of theory change. What is retained, and what we are entitled to believe, is structure, understood by ontic structuralists as modal structure. It sorts the view into an epistemic form, which falls to the Newman/Demopoulos–Friedman triviality result when stated as a Ramsey sentence (§3.2), and an ontic form with seven distinguishable theses, from eliminativism about individuals to "objects are constructs" (§4). The ontic form is motivated by quantum non-individuality and entanglement, gauge symmetry and the hole argument. The entry closes with seven objections, each answered in the author's own voice (§5). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-038](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-038.md)); the standard map of the structural-realism literature, written by the founder of OSR. Read §2 for the theory-change argument, §3.2 for the Newman problem and §4's seven OSR theses. Treat §5's rebuttals as the author's position, not as the field's settled view. |
| [LIT-046](../../record/literature.d/LIT-046.md) v3 | The Computational Foundations of Collective Intelligence | Pilgrim et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2509.07999](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07999). A conceptual framework, with no model, data or derivation. It sets out individual and collective computational resources in four dimensions: sensing ΣI_i, states S_c × ∏S_i, processes F_c + ΣF_i, and actions ∏A_i (Table 1). It then maps the collective's surplus onto familiar mechanisms (aggregation with noise σ_i²/n, distributional beliefs P(H), memory persistence T_c, division of labour). Coordination and cooperation are given as the constraints that make the trade-off, and three animal case studies are retold in these terms. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-055); a clear, well-referenced vocabulary for collective intelligence (Marr's levels run bottom-up). Its central claim, that the known forms of collective intelligence are "aspects of a single unifying principle", is asserted and illustrated, not derived. It would be settled by one case where the resource-and-constraint accounting yields a quantitative prediction that existing mechanism-specific models do not. |
| [LIT-047](../../record/literature.d/LIT-047.md) v3 | Is It Good to Cooperate? Testing the Theory of Morality-as-Cooperation in 60 Societies | Curry et al. (2019), DOI-10.1086/701478. Coding 3,460 eHRAF paragraphs on the 60 Probability Sample Files societies found 962 morally valenced mentions of seven cooperative behaviours, 961 of them positive. The one negative is Chuuk admiration of open theft as dominance. Presence varied widely by domain: property 54/60 (90%), kin 47 (78%), group 44 (73%), reciprocity 43 (72%), respect 35 (58%), bravery 32 (53%), fairness 9 (15%). Presence correlated with ethnographic coverage (r = 0.43). The design can detect a morally positive mention but has almost no way to register a negative one, so the 99.9% is weak evidence for universality. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-034](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-034.md)); the clearest statement of morality-as-cooperation, with an unusually good commentary set (Bloom; Smith & Kurzban; Wong) that states the objections exactly. Its empirical test is weakly diagnostic: the codebook defines the behaviours in virtue terms, the authors' footnote 3 recodes conflicts as cooperation-vs-cooperation, and the first-pass selection was not blind. It would be settled by blind coding of all moralized content in the same sample, testing whether non-cooperative domains (purity, sex, harm) are moralized as often. |
| [LIT-048](../../record/literature.d/LIT-048.md) v2 | Transfer Entropy as a Log-likelihood Ratio | Barnett & Bossomaier (2012), [ARXIV-1205.6339](https://arxiv.org/abs/1205.6339). For a parametric Markov model f(x_t \| x^k_t, y^k_t; θ) of X, the likelihood-ratio estimator T̂ = −(1/(n−k)) log Λ for the null "no dependence on Y's past" converges almost surely to the transfer entropy T_{Y→X} (Theorem 1a). Under the null, 2(n−k)T̂ is asymptotically χ²(d), with d the difference in parameter counts (Theorem 1b). For finite-state chains the ordinary plug-in estimator is exactly this estimator, with d = (a−1)a^k(b^k−1). For a linear Gaussian VAR, T̂ is half the Granger-causality statistic. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-053); a short, correct letter and the standard citation for reading transfer entropy as a log-likelihood ratio. The consistency proof is informal, and the χ² results are cited to Wald (1943) rather than proved, but the identity itself is exact and still the one to cite. |
| [LIT-049](../../record/literature.d/LIT-049.md) v2 | A coarse-graining account of individuality: how the emergence of individuals represents a summary of lower-level evolutionary processes | Bourrat (2023), DOI-10.1007/s10539-023-09917-x. Bourrat proposes that a partition of particles into collectives defines individuals when the "projected" prediction (coarse-grain at t1, then evolve the coarse-grained state) differs from the "truthful" one (evolve at the fine grain, then coarse-grain at t2) by δC = C_T − C_P < θ, across many traits (Eqs. 1–2). He argues that, because measurement and computation are finite, coarse-graining can be the only way to predict at all: in his example, 100 operations predict 10 entities for 10 timesteps or 2 collectives for 50. That indispensability is his whole case for the "quasi-ontological" status of collectives at Godfrey-Smith and Kerr's fifth stage. Nothing is computed on any model. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-064](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-064.md)); a lucid philosophical statement of "individual = a coarse-graining worth its cost". Its formal criterion is informal and, as written, degenerate: minimal δC picks the finest partition, and the cost term the argument depends on is never put into the criterion. It would be settled by a worked ETI model in which an explicit accuracy–cost objective is computed and picks out the collectives. |
| [LIT-050](../../record/literature.d/LIT-050.md) v2 | Mean-Field-Type Game Theory with Rosenblatt Noise | Tembine et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2506.08025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08025). The paper's control and game results use linear state feedback, and they depend on the Rosenblatt noise only through its covariance, which is exactly fBM's with the same H. They are therefore identical to the fBM results. The ergodic LQ gain (Thm 4), K̂ = −[b₁ + √(b₁² + 4H(1−H)b₂²q/r)]/(2b₂(1−H)), is correct and reduces to the Brownian LQR gain at H = ½. But the paper's headline "suboptimality of noise approximation" (§VI-A, repeated for the variance-aware control, zero-sum, non-zero-sum and MFTG settings) holds only against surrogates with a different covariance; against fBM with the same H it is false. The ML claim that Rosenblatt "super-diffusion transformers" "significantly enhance the performance of generative machine intelligence models" (§XIV-C) has no model, training, dataset or experiment behind it anywhere in the paper. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-068); not worth a reader's time. The control-theoretic results are earlier papers' (its refs [43]–[46]) restated. Its central new claim, that Gaussian and fBM surrogates give suboptimal control, is false for fBM with matching H, since the costs depend only on covariance. Its empirical sections are unsourced vignettes: numbers without references, "Rosenblatt shape" fits without any fitting procedure, and data from the first author's own startups. Its ML claim is an assertion with no model or experiment. A reader wanting Rosenblatt-noise LQ control should go to Čoupek–Duncan–Maslowski–Pasik-Duncan (CDC 2018, ref [43]) and Čoupek–Duncan–Pasik-Duncan (SPA 2022, ref [27]). |
| [LIT-051](../../record/literature.d/LIT-051.md) v2 | Lecture Notes on Control System Theory and Design | Başar et al. (2020), [ARXIV-2007.01367](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.01367). A conventional first-year graduate course in linear state-space control. It covers modelling and linearisation, LTI/LTV solutions, Lyapunov stability, controllability and observability with Kalman/Hautus tests and minimal realisations, then pole placement and observers, integral control, sensitivity, and finally HJB/LQR and a heuristic minimum principle. Its most useful non-standard content is the Pendubot thread (Chs 1, 9, 10): an observer-based design from one sensor is fragile (the compensator has an unstable pole at s ≈ 46.4), while full-state LQR has \|S(jω)\| ≤ 1, infinite gain margin and ±60° phase margin. Every worked example I recomputed is correct. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-062); a sound, free, compact state-space control course, worth reading as background for LQR, Lyapunov and HJB when a reader needs one place for them. It is not a source for anything current, and the preface's promise of a reinforcement-learning bridge is not delivered in the text. |
| [LIT-052](../../record/literature.d/LIT-052.md) v2 | SCOTUS allows Trump admin to send eight men to South Sudan with no process | Geidner (2025), <https://www.lawdork.com/p/scotus-allows-trump-admin-to-send>. The post reports that the Supreme Court, "on an apparent 7-2 vote", held that the district court could not enforce its May 21 remedial order for the men held in Djibouti, because the Court had stayed the underlying April 18 injunction. It quotes the Kagan concurrence and the Sotomayor–Jackson dissent accurately. Against the order, its account of the holding is accurate. Its framing (the headline's "with no process", "Permission had been granted", "seven justices are allowing") goes beyond what the order decides. It also omits the majority's reasoning, and it follows the dissent's count of eight men where the per curiam refers to six. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-048](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-048.md)); an accurate secondary account of DHS v. D.V.D., No. 24A1153 (3 July 2025), whose order is the primary source to read and is not held here; its framing goes beyond what the order decides and omits the majority's reasoning. |
| [LIT-053](../../record/literature.d/LIT-053.md) v2 | The Homological Nature of Entropy | Baudot & Bennequin (2015), DOI-10.3390/e17053253. On a finite probability space, take an information structure S (a set of partitions closed under joins that have a common refinement in S and containing the trivial partition) and a probability family Q complete for it. The information cohomology H*(S;Q) is computed by a Hochschild-type bar complex whose action is Shannon's averaged conditioning. If S is connected and "sufficiently rich" and cochains are measurable, then H¹ is one-dimensional and spanned by Shannon entropy (Thm 1). This makes entropy the unique measurable 1-cocycle, up to one multiplicative constant per connected component. In the unitary-invariant quantum case H¹_U = 0: Shannon entropy is the coboundary of minus the von Neumann entropy, which is the unique invariant 0-cochain with that property up to an additive constant (Thm 3). The tree ("arborescent") version again gives H¹ spanned by entropy (Thm 4). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-088](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-088.md)); the founding paper of information cohomology, with complete proofs of the degree-one results (Thms 1, 3, 4). Read it for those. Its broader programme (higher mutual informations as cocycles, super/sub-harmonicity, max-entropy discrimination) is sketched or deferred here to unpublished companion papers. |
| [LIT-054](../../record/literature.d/LIT-054.md) v2 | A complete graphical calculus for Spekkens' toy bit theory | Backens & Duman (2014), [ARXIV-1411.1618](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.1618). Take the maximal-knowledge fragment of Spekkens' toy bit theory with post-selected measurements, viewed as relations over {1,2,3,4}ⁿ. It has a ZX-style red–green graphical calculus that is universal (§4.3), sound (§4.4) and complete (Thm 17). Completeness goes through a proof that the toy theory's binary "check-matrix" formalism coincides with the qubit stabilizer one (valid states SᵀJS = 0, valid transformations QᵀJQ = J, Lemma 3). With it come graph-state/local-complementation results for toy states (Thms 4–5), map–state duality (§5.2) and an rGS-LO normal form (Thms 10–11). The rewrite rules differ from the stabilizer ZX-calculus only through the phase group: Z₂×Z₂ against Z₄. That difference appears as an "11-commutation" rule in place of π-commutation (§4.5). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-066); the completeness result is proved and still the diagrammatic reference for the toy bit theory. Read §2.1, §4.2, §4.5 and §5.1 for the stabilizer comparison. The ψ-epistemic framing in the abstract and §1 is motivation only; the paper does no ψ-epistemic analysis. |
| [LIT-055](../../record/literature.d/LIT-055.md) v2 | Who Goes Nazi? | Thompson (1941), <https://harpers.org/archive/1941/08/who-goes-nazi/>. Thompson's answer is that susceptibility to Nazism is "something in them", not race, nationality, creed or class. She argues it through fourteen character sketches of guests at an imagined New York party. People with an inner code, security or happiness "never go Nazi". The humiliated intellectual, the scared speculator, the spoiled son, the labour boss and the success-chaser "would all go Nazi in a crisis". It is a journalist's typology from experience in Germany, Austria and France, and no data supports it. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-056](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-056.md)); A primary historical text, and the source of a still-circulating heuristic, worth reading as what one experienced anti-fascist observer believed in 1941. It is not evidence for what predicts fascist support. |
| [LIT-056](../../record/literature.d/LIT-056.md) v3 | Consciousness in Artificial Intelligence: Insights from the Science of Consciousness | Butlin et al. (2023), [ARXIV-2308.08708](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.08708). The report assumes computational functionalism and takes a "theory-heavy" approach. From it the authors derive 14 indicator properties: RPT-1–2, GWT-1–4, HOT-1–4, AST-1, PP-1 and AE-1–2. The claim for them is only that more of them raises the probability of consciousness. They assess a handful of systems against subsets of the list and conclude that no current system "appears to be a strong candidate". Transformer LLMs have "only a relatively weak case" for any GWT indicator, because a single forward pass has no recurrence or global broadcast. Perceiver arguably has GWT-1, GWT-2 and half of GWT-4 but lacks broadcast. Among the embodied agents, AdA is the likeliest to meet AE-2. Most indicators could be built with standard ML techniques. The abstract's "no current AI systems are conscious" is stronger than anything the body shows. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-052); the reference statement of the indicator-property ("theory-heavy") approach to AI consciousness, worth reading for §1–§2 (method and theory survey) and Tables 1–2. Its §3.2 system verdicts are architecture-dated and cover only GWT and agency/embodiment. The 2026 Trends in Cognitive Sciences paper by an overlapping 20-author group may now be the citable version; I have not read it, so this should not yet be marked Superseded. |
| [LIT-057](../../record/literature.d/LIT-057.md) v2 | Effect of delay on the emergent stability patterns in generalized Lotka–Volterra ecological dynamics | Saeedian et al. (2021), [ARXIV-2110.11914](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.11914). The paper studies random delayed generalized Lotka–Volterra dynamics, ẋ_i = x_i(r_i + Σ_j b_ij x_j(t−τ)), with instantaneous interactions switched off (A = 0) in every delay result. A feasible, stable equilibrium loses stability by a Hopf bifurcation at τ_c = min over eig(J) of (1/\|j\|) arctan(Re j/Im j) (Eq. 14), where J = diag(−B⁻¹r)B, and this matches simulation (Fig. 5b; S = 100 is stable at τ = 12 and oscillates at τ = 18). In the oscillatory regime, at τ = 20 and 22 for S ≲ 70, community-level CV falls with S while mean species-level CV rises (Fig. 6). The paper reads this as a positive diversity–stability relation but tests it against no averaging null. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-061); A modest, partly numerical preprint whose critical-delay formula is the standard linear delay result applied to a random community matrix. Its diversity–stability claim would be settled by comparing CV_c(S) against the √S averaging expected from weakly correlated oscillators (the "statistical inevitability" null of Tilman et al. 1998, which the paper cites as ref. 40), and by resolving whether the "divergent" phase is numerical or real. |
| [LIT-058](../../record/literature.d/LIT-058.md) v3 | Mutual Information and Minimum Mean-square Error in Gaussian Channels | Guo et al. (2004), [arXiv:cs/0412108](https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0412108). Let Y = √snr·X + N with N ~ N(0,1) independent of X, and let X have any distribution with E X² < ∞. Then d/dsnr I(X; √snr X + N) = ½·mmse(X \| √snr X + N) in nats, equivalently I(snr) = ½∫₀^snr mmse(γ) dγ (Thm 1, Eq. 47). The vector form is d/dsnr I(X; √snr HX + N) = ½·E‖HX − H·E[X\|Y]‖² (Thm 2). In continuous time, the same identity combined with Duncan's I = (snr/2)·cmmse gives cmmse(snr) = (1/snr)∫₀^snr mmse(γ) dγ (Thm 8). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-086); the founding statement and proof of the I-MMSE identity. It is fully proved (five proofs, with the interchange of limits justified in App. IV) and it remains the reference. Read §II-C for the incremental-channel proof and §V-B for the one place it fails (feedback). |
| [LIT-059](../../record/literature.d/LIT-059.md) v3 | The Strongest El Niño Ever | Hausfather (2026), <https://www.theclimatebrink.com/p/the-strongest-el-nino-ever>. Hausfather reports that the July 2026 runs of 14 seasonal models (667 members, model-weighted) put the median peak Niño 3.4 anomaly of the 2026-27 El Niño at 3.6 °C, against his computed record of 2.75 °C (2015-16). That gives about a 91% chance of a record, or about 77% on a relative index where 1982-83 holds the record at 2.69 °C. He cautions that no model has been verified at this intensity. - NOAA's own series agree on the rankings but give lower record values: 2.67 monthly for 2015-16 and 2.6 in ONI. - Observations through mid-September 2026 are on a record-setting track: weekly Niño 3.4 +3.0 °C on 16 Sep, and monthly ERSSTv5 +2.17 in August. The event has not yet peaked, so the forecast cannot yet be verified. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-031](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-031.md)); This is a dated forecast. What settles it is the observed 2026-27 peak, i.e. monthly ERSSTv5 Niño 3.4 and RONI through about February 2027. The observations to mid-September are consistent with a record but do not yet reach 3.6 °C. |
| [LIT-060](../../record/literature.d/LIT-060.md) v2 | The Mothership Vortex: An Investigation Into the Firm at the Heart of the Democratic Spam Machine | Bonica (2025), <https://data4democracy.substack.com/p/the-mothership-vortex-…>. From FEC filings, Bonica reports that a core network of PACs linked to Mothership Strategies raised about $678M from individuals since 2018. Of that, $159M went to Mothership, out of $282M it received from all clients. "At most" $11M reached candidates, campaigns or national party committees, a "fundraising efficiency rate of just 1.6 percent". The headline numbers cannot be reproduced from the published repo: its inputs are missing, it has a fatal undefined variable, and it computes several reported figures nowhere. As documented, its disbursement queries cover only the latest two-year period and at most 1,000 disbursements per committee, largest first, which would bias the $11M downward rather than cap it. The post's own infographic contradicts several of its figures. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-042](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-042.md)); A plausible and important claim about political-fundraising efficiency, not established by what was published. It would be settled by a run that reproduces the figures, with the input files committed, all cycles queried and paginated fully, and independent expenditures and field spending counted explicitly in or out of "reaching campaigns". |
| [LIT-061](../../record/literature.d/LIT-061.md) v2 | Mike Johnson's Institutional Betrayal | Moynihan (2026), <https://donmoynihan.substack.com/p/mike-johnsons-institution…>. Moynihan argues that, in the 2026 DHS appropriations lapse, Speaker Johnson refused a vote on the Senate's bill (which funded DHS except ICE and CBP) and passed a Trump-backed full-DHS bill, then recessed. In doing so he backed a presidential memo directing TSA pay from unspecified funds with a "reasonable and logical nexus to TSA operations". Moynihan calls this ceding Congress's power of the purse. His distinction is that the President may pay staff during a lapse only from an identifiable appropriation, which exists for ICE and CBP (under the One Big Beautiful Bill Act) and not for TSA. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-084](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-084.md)); A clear, well-sourced piece of commentary on one episode of the 2025–26 appropriations conflict, worth reading as argument. Its main factual premise (the memo names no appropriation) checks out against the memo, and its timeline comes from news reports it links. |
| [LIT-062](../../record/literature.d/LIT-062.md) v3 | On the reality of the quantum state | Pusey et al. (2011), [ARXIV-1111.3328](https://arxiv.org/abs/1111.3328). Take pure states \|ψ0⟩, \|ψ1⟩ with \|⟨ψ0\|ψ1⟩\| = cos θ. Assume (a) a model assigns each preparation a distribution µ_i(λ) over physical states, (b) n independent preparations yield the product distribution µ_{x1}(λ1)⋯µ_{xn}(λn), and (c) outcome probabilities depend only on (λ1, …, λn). If the model's predictions are within ε of quantum theory, then D(µ0, µ1) ≥ 1 − 2ε^{1/n} for any n with 2^{1/n} − 1 ≤ tan(θ/2) (Eq. 7, App. B). At ε = 0 the distributions for distinct pure states cannot overlap on a set of non-zero measure, so Harrigan–Spekkens ψ-epistemic models are ruled out. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-030); the founding ψ-ontology theorem. It is short, fully proved (App. A–B), and still the reference that every later ψ-epistemic/ψ-ontic argument, including LIT-007 §I.C and LIT-090, positions itself against. Read it with its assumption (b)–(c) in view, since that is where every response pushes. |
| [LIT-063](../../record/literature.d/LIT-063.md) v2 | The indeterminacy of word segmentation and the nature of morphology and syntax | Haspelmath (2011), DOI-10.1515/flin.2011.002. Haspelmath tests ten morphosyntactic criteria for wordhood and finds that none is necessary and sufficient. No combination picks out what linguists write between spaces, and published test batteries pick criteria opportunistically (Table 1: nine studies, no two with the same battery).
