Hence (41, p. 65): "Linguists have no good basis for identifying words across languages, and hence no good basis for a general distinction between syntax and morphology". A fuzzy "word" would rescue the distinction only if units on the boundness continuum were shown to cluster, and that is untested (§6). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-073](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-073.md)); This is the standard statement of the negative case about the cross-linguistic word, and both [LIT-071](../../record/literature.d/LIT-071.md) and [LIT-031](../../record/literature.d/LIT-031.md) cite it for exactly that; read it with the author's own later constructive follow-up, "Defining the word" (WORD 69(3), 2023, not read here). |
| [LIT-064](../../record/literature.d/LIT-064.md) v3 | Talagrand Meets Talagrand: Upper and Lower Bounds on Expected Soft Maxima of Gaussian Processes with Finite Index Sets | Chu & Raginsky (2025), [ARXIV-2502.06709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06709). Take a centered Gaussian process on a finite set T with max variance σ² and minimum separation a. The expected softmax-weighted average g(β) = E[Σ_t X_t e^{βX_t}/Z] satisfies g(β) ≤ √(2σ²·E D(ν_β‖ν₀)) for every β (Thm 1, D = KL from uniform). For β ≥ β*, g(β) ≥ c·a·√(E D(ν_β‖ν₀)), with c the Sudakov constant (1/17 via Marcus–Rosen) (Thm 2). For i.i.d. N(0,σ²) logits there is an exact identity, g(β) = βσ²(1 − E‖ν_β‖²₂) (Lemma 2). The quenched free energy is bounded above by √(2σ²·E D_{1/2}(ν_β‖ν₀)), with an i.i.d. lower bound of (cσ/2)√(E D_{1/2}) (Thms 4–5). | Superseded — by ANTH-LIT-726; moved to the Anthology of the SOTA as ANTH-LIT-726 on 2026-09-26, at the owner's request, with its Read NOTE; nothing is held in both records (ADR-001) |
| [LIT-065](../../record/literature.d/LIT-065.md) v2 | Grand jury transcript, In re Grand Jury Investigation 25 GJ 994, October 9, 2025 ("Broadview Six") [descriptive title] | U.S. District Court for the Northern District of Illinois et al. (2026), <https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28220756-10925/>. At the 9 Oct 2025 session (Special June 2024 Grand Jury, 25 GJ 994), AUSA Sheri Mecklenburg opened by telling the jurors she had waited for "the Thursday grand jury" because "I know you and I trust you ... and I would never ask you to charge somebody if I didn't think there was probable cause" (tr. 3). The only witness was FBI Special Agent Evan Hylton (tr. 10–61). He answered mostly "Yes" to leading questions: on the driver's second-hand account, on his own photos of the car taken 1 Oct, and on social-media identifications of the six. Jurors then pressed on the damage, the conspiracy theory and whether the driver was yet on duty. One said "I personally feel the complete opposite way" (tr. 75). The vote is not in the transcript; press reports it as a "no bill". | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-033](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-033.md)); This is the complete primary record of the first, reportedly no-billed, presentation. It is the document a reader should consult on the vouching and presentation questions, rather than press paraphrase. |
| [LIT-066](../../record/literature.d/LIT-066.md) v3 | The varieties of contemplative experience: A mixed-methods study of meditation-related challenges in Western Buddhists | Lindahl et al. (2017), DOI-10.1371/journal.pone.0176239. This is a qualitative taxonomy, not a prevalence estimate. Sixty Western Buddhist meditators were recruited for having had meditation-related difficulties, and 32 teachers/clinicians were interviewed. Their accounts were coded into 59 experience categories in 7 domains and 26 influencing-factor categories in 4 domains. The most frequent category was fear/anxiety/panic/paranoia (82%). 73% reported moderate-to-severe impairment in at least one domain, 17% suicidality and 17% inpatient hospitalisation. Median symptom duration was 1–3 years. All of these are frequencies within a sample selected for difficulty, as the authors state. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-036](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-036.md)); the reference taxonomy of challenging meditation-related experiences and worth reading for its codebook and its explicit limits. Its frequencies and its "causality" scoring do not support prevalence or causal claims, and the authors say so. |
| [LIT-067](../../record/literature.d/LIT-067.md) v2 | A Social Equilibrium Existence Theorem | Debreu (1952), DOI-10.1073/pnas.38.10.886. Debreu proves existence of an equilibrium for an "abstract economy" (generalized game), in which each agent's feasible set A_ι(ā_ι) depends on the others' actions. The conditions: each action set 𝔄_ι is a contractible polyhedron; A_ι has a closed graph with non-empty compact values; f_ι is continuous on the graph into the extended reals; the value function φ_ι(ā_ι) = max f_ι is continuous; and each best-response set M_ā_ι is contractible. The proof applies the Eilenberg–Montgomery/Begle fixed-point theorem to the product best-response map. A Remark replaces the joint continuity of φ_ι with continuity (lower hemicontinuity) of A_ι plus a compact graph. A Corollary gives a saddle-point theorem that contains Kakutani's and von Neumann's. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-037](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-037.md)); the original existence theorem for generalized (constrained) games, underlying Arrow–Debreu (1954). Short, fully self-contained, and still the canonical citation. Later results (Shafer–Sonnenschein 1975; the generalized-Nash literature) weaken its hypotheses but do not supersede it as the source. |
| [LIT-068](../../record/literature.d/LIT-068.md) v2 | How Local Separators Shape Community Structure in Large Networks | Frenkel & Carmesin (2025), [ARXIV-2504.14501](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14501). This is a comparison of the authors' local 1- and 2-separator decompositions against Infomap, Label Propagation, Leiden and Louvain (igraph) on five sparse graphs (62 to 9,133 nodes). Local separators score lower overlapping modularity than the modularity optimisers everywhere, and lower than Infomap on 3 of 5 graphs (Table 2). Their edge is confined to high density thresholds on the two road networks, where the best radius gives a handful more qualifying clusters: about 12 against about 5 at β ≥ 1.25 on Euroroads, and about 50 against about 40 for Infomap at β ≥ 1.5 on NRW (Figs. 3–4). On Netscience all methods are similar. At δ = 1 on both road networks, and at δ = 1.25 on NRW, Infomap yields more qualifying clusters than any local-separator setting. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-085); not worth a reader's time. Its critique of modularity (§2.4) contains two false statements. Its own "community detection problem" (§2.5) is defined but never scored. Read at the paper's own thresholds, the figures do not support "consistently identify the densest communities". The method itself is never defined here, so a reader wanting local separators should go to Carmesin & Frenkel, arXiv:2312.12354 (its ref. [9]). |
| [LIT-069](../../record/literature.d/LIT-069.md) v2 | Surface optimization governs the local design of physical networks | Meng et al. (2026), DOI-10.1038/s41586-025-09784-4. The degree counts in Fig. 1d show k = 4 nodes making up 4.6–17.7% of branch points across the six datasets. The pooled 16.6% is 96% human-neuron nodes. Bifurcation angles also fail to peak at 2π/3. From this the authors infer that local branching minimises sleeve surface area under a minimum circumference w, not wire length. For a regular tetrahedron their numerical minimiser merges two bifurcations into a trifurcation at χ = w/r ≈ 0.83, and a thin branch sprouts orthogonally below ρ = w′/w ≈ 0.6. Per-dataset empirical thresholds are ρ_th = 0.52–0.92. The "exact mapping" to string-theory worldsheets covers only the unconstrained minimal-area problem. The SI says the implemented method yields surfaces that are only locally area-minimising (§5). | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-075](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-075.md)); a promising and cleanly falsifiable hypothesis, but the evidence does not yet separate it from simpler accounts. Three things would settle it: recomputing the empirical P(λ) without the SI's trifurcation-merging rule, running the actual surface minimiser (rather than a sigmoid fitted to one tetrahedron) on random terminal sets, and testing the branching-to-sprouting breakpoint against a no-breakpoint null. |
| [LIT-070](../../record/literature.d/LIT-070.md) | Dynamics of collectives with opinionated agents: The case of scrambling connectivity | Somarakis et al. (2016), DOI-10.1109/ECC.2016.7810493. For networks of agents that each have their own independently evolving opinion dynamics and communicate cooperatively with neighbours, non-negative matrix theory gives sufficient conditions for stability and convergence to a common synchronized opinion, for both identical and non-identical agents. The "scrambling" connectivity condition is presumably what the title names; I have not verified this from the full text. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-25; not read in full |
| [LIT-071](../../record/literature.d/LIT-071.md) v2 | The lexicalist hypothesis: Both wrong and superfluous | Bruening (2018), DOI-10.1353/lan.2018.0000. Bruening argues that the lexicalist hypothesis is wrong on three counts. Phrases feed word formation productively (phrasal compounds, zero-derivation from phrases, German resultative nominalizations), ellipsis and focus target both phrases and morphemes, and the claimed principled differences between the word system and the phrase system (locality, adjuncts, headedness, idiosyncrasy) do not hold. It is also superfluous: every restriction it was meant to explain follows from constraints stated over X0 vs XP and from the selectional properties of individual affixes, none of which mention "word". So one combinatorial component should build words and phrases. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-065](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-065.md)); This is the most complete single-article case against lexical integrity from inside generative syntax, and it is the syntactic source [LIT-031](../../record/literature.d/LIT-031.md) cites; read it together with Müller's 2018 reply in the same volume, which disputes its extraction, coordination and resultative arguments. |
| [LIT-072](../../record/literature.d/LIT-072.md) v2 | Iranian Hackers Targeted US Officials in Elaborate Social Media Attack Operation | Lennon (2014), <https://www.securityweek.com/iranian-hackers-targeted-us-officials-ela…>. SecurityWeek relays iSIGHT Partners' claim that more than a dozen fake personas, supported by a fake news site (NewsOnAir.org), ran a social-media espionage campaign from 2011 aimed at US military, diplomatic, congressional, press, think-tank and defence targets. The purpose was credential phishing and reconnaissance. Iranian origin is inferred from targeting, "technical indicators" and a Tehran working-day schedule, and iSIGHT says it has "no information implicating the ultimate sponsor". The article's figure of "over 2,000" is connections to the personas, not confirmed victims. iSIGHT itself says it is "not clear ... how many credentials the attack has captured". | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-058](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-058.md)); a faithful relay of iSIGHT Partners' public NEWSCASTER disclosure (28 May 2014), which is the primary source to read and is not held here; every passage this article attributes to a 'closely held report' is in that public disclosure, and '2,000' counts connections, not victims. |
| [LIT-073](../../record/literature.d/LIT-073.md) v2 | General probabilistic theories: An introduction | Plávala (2021), [ARXIV-2103.07469](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.07469). A self-contained textbook on finite-dimensional GPTs whose central results are that state spaces and effects are dual (K = {φ ∈ A(K)*+ : ⟨φ, 1_K⟩ = 1}, Thm 3.19) and that optimal two-state discrimination succeeds with probability ½(1 + ‖λx₀ − (1−λ)x₁‖) in base norm (Thm 3.43). Every composite lies between the minimal and maximal tensor products (Prop. 5.6), and four statements are equivalent: id_K is measure-and-prepare, id_K is self-compatible, K admits a universal broadcasting channel, and K is a simplex (Thm 7.7); minimal = maximal iff one factor is a simplex is cited to [134], not proved. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-035); the best single entry point to the GPT vocabulary this record's Spekkens-programme readings presuppose (state space, effect algebra, GPT dimension, tomographic locality, minimal/maximal tensor products, measure-and-prepare). Read §2–3, §5.1, §5.5, §6.1–6.5 and §7.1. It is not the place for restricted theories like Spekkens' toy theory, to which §3.7 gives a single paragraph. |

| [LIT-075](../../record/literature.d/LIT-075.md) v3 | Comparing cooperative geometric puzzle solving in ants versus humans | Dreyer et al. (2024), DOI-10.1073/pnas.2414274121. On one T-shaped piano-movers puzzle, the CDF of solved attempts against normalized path length shows large ant groups (≈80 carriers, n=28) well ahead of small groups (≈7, n=48) and a 3-run composite single-ant series. An agent-based model with group-size-independent rules reproduces the difference. Human groups of 6–26 barred from talking or gesturing needed more attempted state transitions than single people (−32% relative to the individuals' median; n=48 groups vs 61 individuals), and talking groups were only slightly better (+7%). No significance test is reported anywhere: "significantly" rests on overlapping-or-not ±1 SE bands. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-040](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-040.md)); worth reading, and unusually well instrumented: force meters on every human handle, a calibrated physics model for the ants, and a like-for-like puzzle across species and scales. Its mechanism stories for the humans (majority vote with greediness P=0.2; "follow a random leader"; groupthink) are fitted or observed informally rather than tested, and should be read as hypotheses. |
| [LIT-076](../../record/literature.d/LIT-076.md) v2 | Performing all-atom molecular dynamics simulations of intrinsically disordered proteins with replica exchange solute tempering | Koneru et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2505.01860](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01860). A how-to chapter for REST2 enhanced sampling of intrinsically disordered proteins in GROMACS 2022.5 with PLUMED 2.9.0, worked on the α-synuclein C-terminal fragment (residues 121–140) with the a99SB-disp force field and water. The example uses 10 replicas, a solute-temperature ladder from 300 K to 450 K, swap attempts every 1.6 ps, and a ≥ ~20 % acceptance target. Its load-bearing methodological point is that high swap acceptance "is not, in isolation, sufficient evidence of robust conformational sampling or convergence". Convergence has to be judged by comparing demultiplexed replicas' structural distributions, with blocking-analysis errors. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-076); A practical, current protocol worth reading for anyone setting up REST2 on an IDP, especially the starting-structure advice and the demultiplexed-replica convergence checks. It is a tutorial, not a benchmark: it reports no quantitative results and makes no comparison with REST3, REHT or tREMD. It also has a few textual errors, listed below; the repository is the ground truth for the example. |
| [LIT-077](../../record/literature.d/LIT-077.md) v2 | What is quantum in quantum randomness? | Grangier & Auffèves (2018), [ARXIV-1804.04807](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.04807). The paper argues informally, with no equations anywhere in it, that an "elementary" system with a fixed finite number N of exclusive modalities, placed in contexts that can change, must behave unpredictably when the context changes: a return to C1 after C2 cannot be certain, or there would be N² > N joint modalities (p. 4). It asserts that adding one condition, that the context can change continuously, makes the N×N modality-transition matrix unistochastic, i.e. Born-rule, but defers that proof to refs [14, 15] (p. 5). The anti-reductionist conclusion follows from Postulate 0, which declares contexts non-contextual by definition, not from an argument against decoherence. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-044); a short, equation-free position paper whose one argument of its own is an informal N = 2 reductio; read it for the position, and read the derivation it cites — Auffèves & Grangier, Sci. Rep. 2017 (arXiv 1610.06164) and arXiv 1801.01398, neither held here — for the result. |
| [LIT-078](../../record/literature.d/LIT-078.md) v2 | Kerr County Commissioners' Court, Regular Session, Monday, June 27, 2016 — verbatim transcript [descriptive title] | Kerr County Commissioners' Court et al. (2016), <https://legacy.co.kerr.tx.us/commcrt/minutes/2016/062716CC.txt>. This is a routine regular session of the Kerr County Commissioners' Court, all motions 4–0. In the reports item (tr. 95–98), Commissioner Moser announced a steering-committee meeting "this Thursday" on a Kerr County flood warning system. He said: - an engineering analysis of the entire system would cost roughly $50,000, possibly split among the County, UGRA and the City; - the county's own spend next budget year might be about $20,000; - Comal County spent about $320,000 on 8 sensor locations plus central command and control; - a countywide system might be "a million dollar thing". Commissioner Baldwin said the old phone chain "still works" and objected to "these damn sirens going off in the middle of night". | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-087](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-087.md)); This is a primary local-government record. The flood-warning exchange is captured verbatim and is the reason to read it. It should be read alongside the follow-up meetings, not alone. |
| [LIT-079](../../record/literature.d/LIT-079.md) v2 | Causal Claims in Economics | Garg & Fetzer (2024), [ARXIV-2501.06873](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06873). GPT-4o-mini is run in nine passes per paper over the first 30 pages of 44,852 NBER/CEPR working papers. Edges kept in at least 4 of 9 passes, JEL-mapped, give "claim graphs" in which the share of edges backed by DiD/IV/RCT/RDD/synthetic control rises from 7.7% (1990) to 31.7% (2020). In year-fixed-effect-only regressions, doubling causal edges is associated with +1.36 pp top-five probability and +11.2% citations, while non-causal counterparts are flat or negative. The validation checks paper-level method labels, and only weakly: RCT and field labels have precision ≤ 0.10. It never checks the edges themselves against human labels. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-083); a promising, openly released claim-extraction infrastructure whose headline associations rest on edges never validated against human labels and on regressions with year fixed effects only. An edge-level human-annotated audit, and outcome regressions with field and method controls on a common sample, would settle it. |
| [LIT-080](../../record/literature.d/LIT-080.md) v2 | Understanding brain states across spacetime informed by whole-brain modelling | Vohryzek et al. (2022), DOI-10.1098/rsta.2021.0247. This is a narrative review with no new data, model or quantitative synthesis. It argues that healthy waking brain dynamics sit in a metastable regime near criticality, which whole-brain models (connectome-coupled Hopf oscillators, weakly coupled, at the edge of instability) reproduce. On its "activity landscape" picture, depression deepens particular attractors and psychedelics flatten the landscape. The depression side rests on three cited fMRI studies, one of them in remitted patients, and the review itself says "further studies will be required". | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-054](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-054.md)); a short, readable map of the Deco/Kringelbach whole-brain-modelling programme and its psychedelic/depression framing, worth reading as an entry point. It is a perspective, not evidence: the landscape picture is a hypothesis resting on small primary studies that it neither sizes nor weighs. |
| [LIT-081](../../record/literature.d/LIT-081.md) v2 | Ensemble Steering, Weak Self-Duality, and the Structure of Probabilistic Theories | Barnum et al. (2009), [ARXIV-0912.5532](https://arxiv.org/abs/0912.5532). A bipartite state ω steers its B-marginal iff ω̂ : [0,u_A] → [0,ω_B] is a strong quotient map of ordered sets (Thm 5.4). When ω̂ is injective and the marginal is interior, a steering state is an order-isomorphism (Cor. 5.5). With Thm 4.1/Cor. 4.2, it follows that universal self-steering makes every irreducible state space homogeneous and weakly self-dual (Prop. 5.8). The step from there to C*-algebraic quantum theory needs strong self-duality, which the paper does not derive, and a Hanche-Olsen correspondence it defers ("as we will establish elsewhere", §6). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-082); a short, readable and mostly proved link from an operational feature (ensemble steering) to cone geometry (homogeneity, weak self-duality). It remains a standard reference for the steering route in GPT reconstructions. Read it with the open weak/strong self-duality gap and the thin argument for Prop. 5.7 in view. |
| [LIT-082](../../record/literature.d/LIT-082.md) v2 | Unification of observational entropy with maximum entropy principles | Schindler et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2503.15612](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15612). The paper defines S^τ_M(ρ) = S(τ) − D_M(ρ‖τ) = −Σ_x p_x log(p_x/V_x), with V_x = Tr(τM_x)e^{S(τ)} (Eqs. 10–12). It is bounded as S(τ) ≥ S^τ_M(ρ) ≥ S(ρ) when ρ obeys the constraint S(ρ;τ) ≤ S(τ) (Eqs. 17–18). It reduces to 24 listed entropies, and it removes traditional observational entropy's infinite-dimensional pathologies whenever S(τ) < ∞ (§VIII.7). Its "second laws" are bounds D_M(ρ̄‖τ) ≤ ε on the time-averaged state, split as equilibration plus prior-fit (Eq. 27). The paper proves the first term small in two regimes (Eqs. 28–29) and the second only under the ETH (Eq. 31). It states that in its own numerical examples the smallness "was [not] proved here analytically" (p. 12). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-043); worth reading as the reference definition of prior-generalised observational entropy and for its catalogue of special cases (§V, Table II). The unification is definitional and its bounds are correct. The second-law programme is a framework plus partial sufficient conditions, not a proved second law for any concrete system. |
| [LIT-083](../../record/literature.d/LIT-083.md) v2 | Toward interoperable representation and sharing of disinformation incidents in cyber threat intelligence | Sánchez González et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2502.20997](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20997). The paper shows that a DISARM-labelled disinformation incident can be encoded in plain STIX 2.1 with four object types (IntrusionSet for the incident, ThreatActor, AttackPattern, Location) and three relationships (uses, attributed-to, targets), then served by an open-source microservice platform (DISINFOX) to OpenCTI through a custom connector. Its validation is a demonstration that 118 hand-labelled incidents passed through the pipeline; it reports no measurement of fidelity, usefulness or scale. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-028); a working, clearly described proof of concept for carrying disinformation incidents as CTI. It is an unreviewed preprint with a deliberately minimal mapping and no evaluation beyond "it ingests"; what would settle it is peer-reviewed publication plus evidence that analysts use the shared incidents, or a comparison with DAD-CDM, which the authors themselves name as the emerging standard. |
| [LIT-084](../../record/literature.d/LIT-084.md) v2 | 3rd EEAS Report on Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference Threats: Exposing the architecture of FIMI operations | European External Action Service et al. (2025), <https://www.eeas.europa.eu/sites/default/files/documents/2025/EEAS-3nd-ThreatReport-March-2025-05-Digital-HD.pdf>. The EEAS classifies channels in foreign information manipulation and interference (FIMI) into four tiers by their tie to a state: official, state-controlled, state-linked (covert, attributed) and state-aligned (non-attributed). It applies the scheme to 505 incidents it detected between 4 Nov 2023 and 4 Nov 2024, involving about 38,000 channels. In a filtered co-involvement graph of 2,055 recurrent channels, 20% are Russian-attributed, 3.5% Chinese-attributed and 76.5% non-attributed. The report states that the sample is illustrative and should not be used to infer general trends. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-060](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-060.md)); worth reading as the primary statement of the EEAS's attribution taxonomy (the Exposure Matrix) and of the covert operations it names. Its figures are counts from an avowedly non-representative monitoring sample, the network method is described only in outline, and several headline numbers need the qualifications recorded here before they are cited. |
| [LIT-085](../../record/literature.d/LIT-085.md) v3 | The No-U-Turn Sampler: Adaptively Setting Path Lengths in Hamiltonian Monte Carlo | Hoffman & Gelman (2011), [ARXIV-1111.4246](https://arxiv.org/abs/1111.4246). NUTS removes HMC's step-count L. It doubles a leapfrog trajectory forwards and backwards in random directions until some balanced subtree satisfies (θ⁺−θ⁻)·r⁻ < 0 or (θ⁺−θ⁻)·r⁺ < 0. It keeps detailed balance by slice sampling and by excluding states that could not have regenerated the tree. With ε set by a dual-averaging scheme (γ = 0.05, t₀ = 10, κ = 0.75, target δ ≈ 0.6), it matched the best hand-tuned HMC in ESS per gradient on two logistic-regression posteriors. It beat that HMC by about 3× on a 250-D Gaussian and on a 3001-D stochastic-volatility model (§4.4). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-081); This is the source of the default sampler in Stan, PyMC and NumPyro, and it is worth reading for its derivation. The algorithm as run today, with multinomial rather than slice selection and adapted mass matrices, is documented elsewhere. A reader who wants current practice should read this paper together with that later account. |
| [LIT-086](../../record/literature.d/LIT-086.md) v2 | The Stochastic-Quantum Correspondence | Barandes (2023), [ARXIV-2302.10778](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.10778). Take any N×N stochastic transition matrix Γ(t←0) on a configuration space. Writing it entrywise as Γ_ij = \|Θ_ij\|² = tr(Θ†P_iΘP_j) (Eq. 15) is always possible, and the paper calls it the stochastic-quantum dictionary. Choosing Θ unitary (by Stinespring dilation to ≤N³ configurations, §3.4) makes Γ unistochastic. The Born rule in the configuration basis (Eq. 23), the Schrödinger equation (Eq. 35, with H ≡ iħ(∂U/∂t)U†) and interference (Eq. 43, the gap between Γ(t←0) and \|U(t←t′)\|²\|U(t′←0)\|²) then follow by definition or short algebra. The Born rule for non-diagonal observables (Eq. 78) is derived by reading a measuring device's configuration, given a composite unitary that produces Eq. (73). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-080); the clearest single statement of the indivisible-stochastic-process reading of quantum theory, and its measurement model (§4) is worth reading. Discount its "new result" labels: the dictionary is an identity, the Schrödinger equation is the definition of H, and unitarity is a representational choice the paper's own gauge footnote does not preserve. |
| [LIT-087](../../record/literature.d/LIT-087.md) v2 | Assessing grand narratives of economic inequality across time | Feinman et al. (2025), DOI-10.1073/pnas.2400698121. Across a global archaeological house-size compilation (SiteGiniLevel: 1,176 sites, 47,019 houses), site Gini coefficients rise only weakly with the number of political levels (r² = 0.16 all sites, 0.22 apex, 0.27 H50) and with log polity population (r² = 0.11, 0.21, 0.08; values printed on Fig. 2, not in the text). The spread widens at three or more levels. A 0–3 expert-coded autocracy score does about as well (r² = 0.10, 0.18, 0.32; Fig. S4), and its sum with levels ("Hierarchical Clout") reaches r² = 0.30 (apex), 0.42 (H50) and 0.50 (H100). All tests are bivariate OLS fits with no controls and no correction for many sites sharing polity-level values. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-049](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-049.md)); worth reading as the widest quantitative record of premodern wealth disparity and a clear rebuttal of deterministic scale-drives-inequality narratives, a negative claim the scatterplots support well. Its positive claim that governance "matters" rests on bivariate r² from an expert-coded index whose "architecture" dimension overlaps with the house-size outcome, and should be read as suggestive. |
| [LIT-088](../../record/literature.d/LIT-088.md) v2 | The Russian perspective on information warfare: conceptual roots and politicisation in Russian academic, political, and public discourse | Fridman (2017), DOI-10.30966/2018.riga.2.3. Fridman argues that the Russian narrative of a "Western information war against Russia" draws on three independently developed theories: Messner's subversion-war (myatezhevoyna), Dugin's net-centric war and Panarin's information war. In his account, politicisation of the narrative was not led by the Kremlin. It is a mutually reinforcing process: scholars promote it, the government writes it into doctrine and law, and a public receptive to it absorbs it. He cites Levada polling from December 2014 in which 87% saw the West as hostile and 42% said the hostility took the form of information war. His conclusion is that the truth lies "somewhere in the middle" between top-down manipulation and bottom-up demand. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-070](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-070.md)); worth reading as a compact, well-footnoted intellectual history of the Russian "information war against Russia" discourse, drawn from Russian-language academic sources. Its account of who drives the politicisation is interpretive, and its chronological argument against Kremlin direction is weaker for Dugin, whose net-centric concept dates from 2007. |
| [LIT-089](../../record/literature.d/LIT-089.md) v2 | Software engineering job openings hit five-year low? | Orosz (2025), <https://blog.pragmaticengineer.com/software-engineer-jobs-five-year-lo…>. Orosz reports that US software-development postings on Indeed stood at 65% of the pre-pandemic baseline, "3.5x" below the 2022 peak and 8% down year on year, while all postings were +10%. He attributes most of the fall to the end of zero interest rates, gives GenAI a secondary "wait and see" role, and doubts Indeed's coverage. - Against Indeed's current data the headline US figures reproduce: 65.2, peak ratio 3.59×, −8.3% y/y, all postings 109.5. - Several side figures and one comparative claim do not reproduce (see corrections). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-039](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-039.md)); its headline figures reproduce from Indeed Hiring Lab's postings series, which is the primary source and is not held here; several secondary claims (the sector comparison, the hospitality figure, the peak month) do not. |
| [LIT-090](../../record/literature.d/LIT-090.md) v3 | Could wavefunctions simultaneously represent knowledge and reality? | Hance et al. (2021), [ARXIV-2101.06436](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.06436). The paper argues, informally and without formal results, that Harrigan–Spekkens' ψ-ontic/ψ-epistemic are contradictories only by stipulation. On the informal senses, "represents reality" and "represents knowledge", a wavefunction can be both. It offers a weaker sufficient condition for "epistemic" (Eq. 6: for some ψ, A, S, the response function A(S\|λ) is not equal to the Born probability p^ψ_A(S) throughout Λψ), which non-overlapping models can meet. It concludes that PBR "shows that wavefunctions cannot overlap on state space, but it says nothing about whether they are epistemic in the broader sense", so it "does not rule out all epistemic models" (§V). | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-072); a short, clear statement that ψ-epistemic in the Harrigan–Spekkens sense is narrower than "epistemic", worth reading for §III–V as a terminological caution when citing PBR. Its positive criterion (Eq. 6) is weak, since it amounts to predictive incompleteness of ψ. Its Appendix A contains claims that the record's own readings contradict. |
| [LIT-091](../../record/literature.d/LIT-091.md) v2 | Networks beyond pairwise interactions: Structure and dynamics | Battiston et al. (2020), [ARXIV-2006.01764](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.01764). The review sets out the representation hierarchy for group interactions (graph and clique complex, bipartite graph, motifs, simplicial complex, hypergraph) with their operators (incidence, adjacency tensor, boundary B_k, Hodge Laplacian L_k = B_kᵀB_k + B_{k+1}B_{k+1}ᵀ with ker L_k ≅ H_k). It surveys the random models and the dynamics built on them. Its own §V makes the key qualification: linear diffusion with three-body terms "can always be reduced to" a weighted pairwise Laplacian (Eq. 35). A random walk with edge-independent vertex weights is equivalent to one on a weighted projected graph (Chitra–Raphael). Qualitatively new node-level behaviour needs nonlinear group coupling or edge-dependent weights. With those, it reports explosive or abrupt transitions and bistability: Kuramoto with triadic coupling, simplicial SIS with a discontinuous transition, and higher-order ecological models that stabilise coexistence. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-057); the standard entry point to higher-order network science, worth reading for §II–§III (definitions and operators) and as a map of the 2020 literature. Read it for definitions and pointers, not as evidence: the dynamical claims are one-paper summaries, several formulas carry typos (listed below), and the conclusion's "profoundly change" list includes linear cases that §V itself shows reduce to pairwise. |
| [LIT-092](../../record/literature.d/LIT-092.md) v2 | DOJ Changes Course | Vance (2026), <https://joycevance.substack.com/p/doj-changes-course>. Vance reports that on 3 Mar 2026, the day after moving to voluntarily dismiss its appeals in the consolidated law-firm executive-order cases (D.C. Cir. No. 25-5241), DOJ filed a "Motion To Withdraw Motion To Voluntarily Dismiss Appeals", over the firms' opposition. She calls the reversal "enormously unusual" and argues it shows DOJ serving "the whims of the president" at a cost to its credibility and the presumption of regularity. She gives further examples from Minnesota habeas contempt proceedings, the Epstein files and Senator Tillis's call for Secretary Noem to resign. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-063](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-063.md)); a short commentary on DOJ's 3 March 2026 motion in the D.C. Circuit (No. 25-5241) to withdraw its motion to dismiss the law-firm executive-order appeals; the docket is the primary source and is not held here. |
| [LIT-093](../../record/literature.d/LIT-093.md) v2 | Notes on the Multiplicative Ergodic Theorem | Filip (2017), [ARXIV-1710.10694](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.10694). Expository notes that prove the Oseledets theorem in four ways. §2 uses projective invariant measures and a splitting lemma, without Kingman. §3.5 checks Kaimanovich's regularity criterion in the symmetric space GLₙ/Oₙ, using Kingman. §4 proves the Karlsson–Ledrappier horofunction theorem (for µ-a.e. ω there is a horofunction h_ω with −(1/N)h_ω(g_ω⋯g_{T^{N−1}ω}x₀) → l) for isometries of proper metric spaces. §5 gives an L² "mean" version via Monod's direct integrals of CAT(0) spaces and Karlsson–Margulis tracking, with an L² mean Kingman theorem. Two of the notes' own derivations do not work as written: Proposition 3.4.8(iii) is mis-stated, and the Lemma 5.1.5 step in the tracking proof does not apply as claimed. The main theorems are standard and not in doubt. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 (NOTE-078); a good, short guide to the geometric view of Lyapunov exponents (symmetric spaces, horofunctions, CAT(0) direct integrals), worth reading for the §2 and §4 proofs. Read Kaimanovich's theorem (proved here only for H²), Prop. 3.4.8 and the §5.3 tracking proof critically, and do not use it as a source of ML claims; it makes none. |
| [LIT-094](../../record/literature.d/LIT-094.md) v2 | Airbnb and its potential impact on the London housing market | Shabrina et al. (2021), DOI-10.1177/0042098020970865. Using Inside Airbnb data, "possible misuse" is defined as entire-home listings available for more than 180 days a year whose host has 2 or more listings. On that definition, about 0.16% of London's housing stock (≈5,300 homes) was possibly misused in 2018, rising to 3.5–7% in a few central LSOAs. Across LSOAs, misuse correlates weakly with purpose-built flats (r = +0.28), private renting (+0.33) and low dwelling-type entropy (−0.14). In a 33-borough OLS with 4 predictors, logged 2015 misuse has β = 0.084 (p = 0.001) on a min–max-normalised 2015–17 rent change. The paper translates this as "a 100% increase … up to 8%" in rent, which is £90 a year. | Active — read in full 2026-09-25 ([NOTE-071](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-071.md)); worth reading as a descriptive map of possibly non-compliant Airbnb supply in London and for its misuse definition. Its rent result is a 33-observation cross-sectional association whose "8% per doubling" translation does not follow from the reported model, and it should not be cited as an effect size. |
| [LIT-095](../../record/literature.d/LIT-095.md) | Two-Level Type Theory and Applications | Annenkov et al. (2017), [ARXIV-1705.03307](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.03307); Mathematical Structures in Computer Science 2023. Two-level type theory (2LTT) pairs a fibrant inner theory (HoTT) with an outer theory with strict equality, so that coherence towers such as semisimplicial types can be stated internally. Basic 2LTT is conservative over HoTT (Props 2.18–2.19): an inner type inhabited in 2LTT is already inhabited in HoTT. A fibrant-replacement type former would force UIP on the inner level (Thm 2.20). | Active — read in part on 2026-09-26, alongside the nLab page it supersedes ([LIT-040](../../record/literature.d/LIT-040.md)) — §1–3, §4.7 and §5 in full, §4.3–4.6 at the level of statements; no NOTE yet, so a full reading is still owed |
| [LIT-096](../../record/literature.d/LIT-096.md) | What Is It Like to Be a Bat? | Nagel (1974), [DOI-10.2307/2183914](https://doi.org/10.2307/2183914). An organism has conscious states if and only if there is something it is like to be that organism. That subjective character belongs to a type of point of view, and it is left out by every functional, intentional or causal analysis, since each is compatible with its absence. Reduction elsewhere moves away from a point of view toward the objective; here that move leads away from the thing being explained. So physicalism may be true, but we have no conception of how it could be. Nagel proposes an "objective phenomenology" that would describe experience to beings who cannot have it. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-089); the source of the "something it is like" criterion of consciousness that the record's AI-consciousness work (LIT-056) adopts as its target, and of the argument that no functional analysis can settle whether that criterion is met. |
| [LIT-097](../../record/literature.d/LIT-097.md) v3 | The Extended Mind | Clark & Chalmers (1998), DOI-10.1093/analys/58.1.7. The paper argues from a functionalist parity claim that a process or standing state which would count as cognitive if it happened in the head counts as cognitive when an external resource carries it, provided the resource is actively coupled to the organism here and now. Otto's notebook entry is its example of such a standing belief. From this it infers, in one paragraph, that the self extends too. The four "criteria" for a coupled resource (constancy, direct availability, automatic endorsement, and the admittedly "arguable" past conscious endorsement) are offered as the features that make the paradigm case clear. The paper explicitly says they are not necessary and sufficient conditions. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-180](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-180.md)); it is worth reading as the source text of the extended-mind thesis, and it is short. Its constitutive conclusion follows from a role-functionalism about belief and a parity principle that the paper states rather than defends, so a reader should take it as the origin of the debate and not as its settlement. |
| [LIT-098](../../record/literature.d/LIT-098.md) v2 | Health and Disease Concepts Cannot Be Grounded in Social Justice Alone | Veit (2025), DOI-10.52685/cjp.25.73.7. Kukla's "institutional definition" makes a condition a health condition iff medicalising it would be best for collective wellbeing. Veit argues that any purely normativist definition of this kind defeats itself for two reasons. First, leaving "social justice" undefined lets a majority with other values medicalise what Kukla wanted to protect. Second, unless the institution of medicine is fixed from outside, the medical toolkit co-evolves until "health would simply become justice" (p. 15). His remedy is pluralism: at least three separate accounts, with justice linked to health empirically, not conceptually. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-097](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-097.md)); worth reading as a short, clearly structured critique of moral conceptual engineering applied to health, though its two objections rest on informal argument and a thought experiment, not on an engagement with Kukla's replies. |
| [LIT-099](../../record/literature.d/LIT-099.md) v2 | Framing Effects in Object Perception | Ivy & Mroczko-Wąsowicz (2025), DOI-10.1007/s13164-024-00763-8. A conceptual proposal with no new data. "Perceptual frames" are learned adaptations of the perceptual system, installed diachronically, often top-down, and then operating bottom-up. They let perceivers given identical input individuate different perceptual objects (the Bach motif, the radiologist's tumour, the Teufel et al. two-tone image). So cognition shapes the perceptual *system* even if it is left open whether any single percept is penetrated. Support is a survey of others' findings (Ernst & Banks 2002 cue weighting, prism adaptation, Teufel et al. 2018, expert anticipation) read through the frame concept. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-150](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-150.md)); worth reading as a clear, short statement of a "system-level versus episode-level" way through the cognitive-penetrability debate, provided the reader knows that its empirical sections interpret others' results and do not test the frame concept against its rivals. |
| [LIT-100](../../record/literature.d/LIT-100.md) v2 | Quantum States for Primitive Ontologists: A Case Study | Belot (2011), DOI-10.1007/s13194-011-0024-8. Inside primitive-ontology Bohmian mechanics, Belot compares four readings of the quantum state: a field on configuration space, a multi-field on 3-space, a law, and a history of velocity dispositions of the particle system. Every reading except the multi-field carries a hard open question: why the world looks 3-dimensional, whether laws can be time-indexed, and whether dispositions lose content. The last is shown by the particle in a box, where every energy eigenstate φn = sin(πnx) induces the same at-rest disposition. The orthodox should prefer the multi-field reading; Belot, on van Fraassen's view of interpretation, prefers to keep the others alive. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-110](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-110.md)); worth reading. It is short, it originated the dispositionalist reading, and the particle-in-a-box counterexample and the analysis of the costs of ψ-as-law are still the points the later literature ([LIT-204](../../record/literature.d/LIT-204.md), [LIT-200](../../record/literature.d/LIT-200.md), [LIT-164](../../record/literature.d/LIT-164.md)) cites. |
| [LIT-101](../../record/literature.d/LIT-101.md) v2 | Moral disciplining: The cognitive and evolutionary foundations of puritanical morality | Fitouchi et al. (2021), DOI-10.1017/S0140525X22002047. Puritanical norms (condemning lust, gluttony, intoxicants, immodesty, idleness; praising temperance and piety) are argued to be cooperative morality after all: people believe cooperation needs self-control and that indulgence erodes it (and discipline trains it), so a single harm/fairness-sensitive moral system condemns victimless pleasures as indirect, probabilistic threats to cooperation. Its decline in rich societies is put down to people seeing others as already self-controlled and trustworthy. The theory is explicitly agnostic about whether the norms actually work. Its most direct support is the authors' own vignette studies (N > 1,100), in which perceived loss of self-control mediated 100% of the perceived effect of indulgence on cooperativeness. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-132](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-132.md)); worth reading as a clearly stated, falsifiable, well-sourced BBS target theory of why "purity" norms exist. Much of its evidence is correlational or the authors' own vignettes, and several predictions (intoxicants, piety) are explicitly untested. |
| [LIT-102](../../record/literature.d/LIT-102.md) v2 | Pregeometry, Formal Language and Constructivist Foundations of Physics | Arsiwalla et al. (2023), [ARXIV-2311.03973](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.03973). A programmatic essay with no construction, theorem or model of its own. It argues that inferential justification in a materialist ontology regresses without end (§3), that a "coherentist" constructivism escapes the regress, and that Wheeler's pregeometry is therefore best read as "structureless structure": the types and compositional rules of a computational language such as homotopy type theory (§§4, 6). Every construction of space from such rules that it points to is in the authors' own earlier papers ([13, 17, 18]) or is promised as "a forthcoming publication" (fn. 1, p. 3). | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-182); not worth a reader's time as an argument for its thesis. The step from the regress problem to formal languages is asserted, not argued, and its one physics claim (that "widespread consensus" holds spacetime to be fundamentally discrete, p. 5) is overstated. A reader wanting pregeometry is better served by the sources it cites: Meschini, Lehto & Piilonen's "Geometry, pregeometry and beyond" ([63]) for the history, and Döring & Isham's topos programme ([36–40]) for a worked formal-language approach. |
| [LIT-103](../../record/literature.d/LIT-103.md) v3 | Closing the Cartesian Bureau de Change | Frankish (2025), DOI-10.1177/10597123251359198. An illusionist's commentary on Veit's *A Philosophy for the Science of Animal Consciousness*. Frankish places Veit's function-first, evolutionary programme in a "post-Cartesian" (qualia-eliminativist) paradigm. He then argues that two things drag it back towards Cartesianism: Veit keeps the words "qualia" and "phenomenal", and he writes as if hedonic valence motivates action "in virtue of its feel". The second implies "a tiny one-dimensional Cartesian bureau de change" that leaves the hard problem "as hard as ever". Because the two paradigms are "incommensurable" (p. 20), Frankish urges Veit to commit to a thoroughly functionalist account. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-143](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-143.md)); worth reading as a five-page statement of the illusionist case against residual "feel" talk in evolutionary accounts of valence, and best read together with Veit's reply ([LIT-105](../../record/literature.d/LIT-105.md)), which answers it point by point. It argues rather than demonstrates: the central premise, that anything causally efficacious is functionally definable and so cannot be irreducibly subjective, is asserted in one paragraph (p. 18). |
| [LIT-104](../../record/literature.d/LIT-104.md) v3 | Philosophy of Mathematics from Descartes to Kant | Carson (2026), DOI-10.1017/9781108982405. The early-modern debate over whether metaphysics can be demonstrated with geometric certainty turned on how Euclidean demonstration secures certainty, and the Element traces the answers from Descartes through Locke, Leibniz, Wolff, Lambert and Mendelssohn to Kant. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found the Element is not open access (Cambridge Core shows "Get access" with purchase temporarily unavailable; Crossref gives only the standard Cambridge licence). Print publication 2026-08-31. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-105](../../record/literature.d/LIT-105.md) v3 | On the Evolution, Science, and Metaphysics of Consciousness | Veit (2025), DOI-10.1177/10597123251413204. Veit's reply to five commentaries on *A Philosophy for the Science of Animal Consciousness* (2023). Its main new content is the metaphysics the book left to one paragraph. He says hedonic "feel" is *identical* with a valence system: "a common currency of sharing evaluative information across multiple control systems". The currency metaphor, he says, is "not Cartesian but cybernetic" (p. 10). He keeps the word "qualia", arguing that Cartesians have no "definitional rights" (p. 9), and he insists the pathological complexity thesis (PCT) is compatible with any metaphysics, dualism and panpsychism included (p. 9). Against his other critics, he defends a bottom-up evolutionary method that uses human data (de Weerd); a naturalism that is neither autopoietic nor biopsychist (Sachs); evaluation-first over prediction-first, consciousness as an adaptation, and graded, partial unity (Suzuki); and the link to welfare science (Chincarini). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-187](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-187.md)); worth reading as the only extended statement of the metaphysics behind the pathological complexity thesis, and as the direct answer to Frankish ([LIT-103](../../record/literature.d/LIT-103.md)). The reply to Frankish is the substantive part. The replies to de Weerd and Suzuki mostly restate the book's positions against objections quoted only in part, so read them alongside the commentaries. |
| [LIT-106](../../record/literature.d/LIT-106.md) | Brandom's Inferentialist Theory and the Meaning Entitlement Connection | Marabini (2015), DOI-10.12816/0028065. Boghossian's objection to substantive, entitlement-based theories of meaning (drawn from pejoratives like "Boche") works against Dummett but not against Brandom. Brandom's material inferences are non-monotonic and ceteris paribus, so one can grasp a concept while refusing to endorse the inferences it licenses. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26 (the skim read PhilArchive's copy through the Wayback Machine); a close reading was attempted the same day and could not fetch it: the Wayback Machine reset its connections, PhilArchive refused the request, and the publisher's reader (Al-Manhal) needs a sign-in. The article is marked open access, so this is an access failure, not a paywall. It stays Deferred until it can be fetched. |
| [LIT-107](../../record/literature.d/LIT-107.md) v3 | Quasi-set theory: a formal approach to a quantum ontology of properties | Holik et al. (2021), DOI-10.1007/s11229-022-03884-8. The paper argues, with no new theorem, that the Lombardi–da Costa ontology of properties for quantum mechanics can be stated in Krause's quasi-set theory Q. On that ontology a system is a bundle of I-type-properties (instances of universal type-properties, represented by the algebra of observables) with no principle of individuality. For each property instance, the indiscernible instances form a qset U([Π]). An atomic bundle is any qset x with qc(x ∩ U) = 1 for each U; the Axiom of Choice in Q guarantees one exists. The bundle's kind is Q(B) = {x ∈ P(Q) \| R(x)}, whose members are all indiscernible. N particles are a subqset N(B) ⊂ Q(B) with qc = N, a definite number with no labels (§6, pp. 19–22). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-127](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-127.md)); worth reading as a short, clear bridge between the modal-Hamiltonian "ontology of properties" (Lombardi et al.) and quasi-set theory (French & Krause 2006). The new formal content is about two pages (§6). Most of §5 restates Q's axioms, and the printed proofs contain errors. It is a useful statement of the position, not a result. |
| [LIT-108](../../record/literature.d/LIT-108.md) v2 | Philosophy of Mathematics and Natural Science | Weyl (1949), DOI-10.1515/9781400833337. Philosophy of mathematics and philosophy of physics are intertwined, with advances in philosophy led by scientific discovery. Weyl's two-part treatment (mathematics, then natural science), drawing on Descartes, Galileo, Hume, Kant, Leibniz and Newton, is a classic mathematician-physicist's statement of that view. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found the book in copyright with no open copy: every Internet Archive scan (the 1949, 1959 and 1963 printings) is lending-only, and the De Gruyter e-book is paywalled. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-109](../../record/literature.d/LIT-109.md) v2 | Dispositions without Conditionals | Vetter (2014), DOI-10.1093/mind/fzu032. Proposes, and explicitly does not claim to have shown superior, a "possibility conception" of dispositions — a disposition is individuated by its manifestation alone ("a disposition to M, full stop") and its modality is possibility, graded for terms like 'fragile' as manifestation in a sufficient proportion of maximally varied cases (centred worlds), and plain possibility for terms like 'breakable'; the defence rests on the semantics of '-able' adjectives and a COCA survey (226 relevant uses of 'disposed to', none ascribing a philosophers' disposition to an inanimate object). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-125](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-125.md)); worth reading as the founding statement of the possibility conception of dispositions (developed in Vetter's Potentiality, OUP 2015); it is an agenda-setting proposal whose comparative case against the conditional conception is deferred to other work, and the reader should hold it to that. |
| [LIT-110](../../record/literature.d/LIT-110.md) v3 | Unknotting reciprocal causation between organism and environment | Baedke et al. (2021), DOI-10.1007/s10539-021-09815-0. The paper gives an epistemic, not a metaphysical, tool for organism–environment reciprocal causation. The loop is "unrolled" into two ordered series of states, O_n and E_n, joined by interventionist (Woodward) causal arrows, and an experienced-environment node Ex_n is added as the interface. Its authors argue that this one diagram keeps an epistemically set organism–environment boundary and maps constitutive, mediational, relational, relocational and physical niche construction onto shared causal patterns. They support this with worked cases, including two multi-species ones (bumblebee–eggplant; rumen microbes–ruminant host). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-111](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-111.md)); worth reading as the clearest statement of the "keep the boundary, unroll the loop" position in the niche-construction reciprocity debate, and as the natural counterpart to Hazelwood ([LIT-156](../../record/literature.d/LIT-156.md)); its model is illustrative, not formalized or tested, and the formalization it promises is deferred to later work. |
| [LIT-111](../../record/literature.d/LIT-111.md) v4 | AI Consciousness: A Centrist Manifesto | Birch (2025), DOI-10.31234/osf.io/af7c9_v1. Birch argues that two challenges must be met together: users misattributing human-like consciousness to chatbots (Challenge One), and possible "profoundly alien" AI consciousness that current science cannot settle (Challenge Two). For Challenge One he offers a personal-identity argument that the chatbot "friend" is typically a character with no persisting bearer (the "persisting interlocutor illusion", §4) and three nudges that are neither "brainwashing" nor "lobotomizing" (§7). For Challenge Two he argues that behavioural markers are "gamed" (§10), that architectural markers are "Janus-faced" (§11), and that the resulting "two-level deadlock" between computational functionalism and biological naturalism can be broken over the long run by comparative animal studies (§12, Fig. 4). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-183](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-183.md)); worth reading as the clearest statement of the "centrist" position, and it coins four concepts the AI-consciousness literature now uses (persisting interlocutor illusion, gaming problem, Janus problem, two-level deadlock). It is a manifesto: its design nudges are untested proposals, and its way out of the deadlock is a research programme that has "barely begun" (§12). |
| [LIT-112](../../record/literature.d/LIT-112.md) v2 | What motivates mental fictionalism? | Drayson (2025), DOI-10.1080/09515089.2025.2577403. Drayson argues that Toon's two motivations for mental fictionalism both fail. The negative argument needs "committed to internal, intentional, causal states ⇒ committed to the representational theory of mind" (her Premise 2), which is false because non-representational functionalism (Lewis, Putnam, Loar) supplies such states. And Toon's positive argument, that pretence lets us genuinely assert that a person is in an overall state S that causes their behaviour, gives him a reason to identify S with the belief and so to be a realist rather than a fictionalist. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-117](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-117.md)); worth reading as a short, well-aimed critique that locates exactly where the case for mental fictionalism (Toon 2023) depends on equating realism about propositional attitudes with the representational theory of mind; it is a commentary on one book and argues against a motivation, not against fictionalism's coherence. |
| [LIT-113](../../record/literature.d/LIT-113.md) v3 | Duality and ontology | Le Bihan & Read (2018), DOI-10.1111/phc3.12555. A survey that sorts realist responses to dualities along two axes. The first is which of the naively interpreted dual worlds count as candidates for actuality: all of them (underdetermination), one (discrimination) or none (nihilism), each with or without the common-core world. That gives options (1)–(6), with the common-core approach being (1), (3) and (5). The second axis is what replaces the common core: an overarching theory, or "pluralism", on which all dual structures are co-instantiated as parts of one world. Its verdicts are conditional: no option breaks the underdetermination without a further metaphysical principle, such as the PII, for which the authors see no independent support. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-152](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-152.md)); worth reading as the shortest clear map of the realist options for interpreting dualities. It is a nontechnical survey, not an argument for any option. Its original contributions (the discrimination/common-core overlap, a defence of discrimination for AdS/CFT, and pluralism) are presented as options, not established. |
| [LIT-114](../../record/literature.d/LIT-114.md) v2 | Philosophy of Statistical Mechanics | Frigg & Werndl (2023), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/statphys-statmech/>. The entry argues that statistical mechanics has no canonical formalism but three umbrellas — Boltzmannian SM, the Boltzmann Equation and Gibbsian SM — and that every live account of the approach to equilibrium (ergodic, typicality, Mentaculus, long-run residence time, coarse-graining, interventionist, epistemic) survives Loschmidt's and Zermelo's objections only by giving up strict irreversibility or adding something outside the isolated dynamics; it closes on the unresolved "schism" that physicists compute in GSM while philosophers explain in BSM. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-103](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-103.md)); worth reading as the current authoritative map of the foundations of SM, with the caveat that it is written by two partisans who present their own long-run residence time account (§4.7) and their "BSM fundamental, GSM effective" view (§6.7) alongside the rivals. |
| [LIT-115](../../record/literature.d/LIT-115.md) v5 | Duality as a category-theoretic concept | Corfield (2015), DOI-10.1016/j.shpsb.2015.07.004. A short programmatic paper. Its thesis is that category theory, not set theory, is the framework in which the scattered instances of mathematical duality can be drawn together. The constructions in §3 (dual adjunctions restricting to dual equivalences on fixed points, truth-value-enriched Galois connections, Isbell conjugation, dualising objects "keeping summer and winter homes") all "revolve around" adjunction, though Corfield expects no "single monolithic account" (p. 59–60). On his usage, 'duality' is reserved for "involution with some form of structural reversal" (p. 57), so several physicists' dualities (homological mirror symmetry as an A∞-equivalence, mirror symmetry's Z2 on the Hodge diamond, S-duality as a vestige of SL(2,Z)) are better called equivalences or symmetries. He proposes, without argument beyond the examples, that philosophers of physics will need homotopy type theory rather than predicate logic for the sameness questions dualities raise. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-141](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-141.md)); worth reading, briefly, as the mathematician-philosopher's counterweight to the physics-first accounts of duality this record holds: it is the clearest statement that what physicists call a duality is often what a mathematician calls an equivalence, and it is only seven pages. Its positive programme (HoTT for philosophy of physics) is announced, not carried out. |
| [LIT-116](../../record/literature.d/LIT-116.md) v3 | The Desperate Search for Meaning in Life | Browning & Veit (2022), <https://philarchive.org/archive/BROWAW-9>. A popular-philosophy chapter that reads Evangelion's characters as seeking meaning first in piloting (duty, recognition), then in connection with others, frustrated by Schopenhauer's porcupine (hedgehog) dilemma. It argues that the failure of Instrumentality shows that a self, and so meaning, exists only through contrast with and separation from others: "without it [otherness], the self cannot exist" (p. 9). Meaning comes from human connection "but maintaining some distance" (p. 10). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-119](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-119.md)); worth reading only as a short, accessible illustration of the relational view of the self (self constituted by the self–other boundary) applied to meaning in life. It offers no original argument, cites nothing beyond two quoted sources, and misdescribes Heidegger's "Dasein". |
| [LIT-117](../../record/literature.d/LIT-117.md) v2 | Agency, Shmagency: Why Normativity Won't Come from What Is Constitutive of Action | Enoch (2006), DOI-10.1215/00318108-2005-014. Constitutivism cannot ground normativity. Even granting that action has constitutive aims, one can ask what reason there is to be an agent rather than a "shmagent" (a near-agent lacking those aims), and the constitutive facts cannot answer that question without presupposing normative reasons. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and no full text could be obtained (SSRN, Duke UP, PhilPapers, ResearchGate and the author's pages refused; JSTOR is closed). A Wayback Machine snapshot of an old Semantic Scholar PDF exists (web.archive.org/web/20200228092518/https://pdfs.semanticscholar.org/d0d1/80b87d01492d9ddcdd487c81ad31466f404c.pdf) but was unreachable from here. It stays Deferred until read. |
| [LIT-118](../../record/literature.d/LIT-118.md) v2 | A Pragmatist Theory of Evidence | Reiss (2015), DOI-10.1086/681643. Reiss proposes an "eliminativist hypothetico-contextualist" (EHC) theory of evidence for type-level causal claims in biomedical and social science. Support comes in two kinds: direct support (patterns we are "entitled to expect" if h is true) and indirect support (patterns incompatible with relevant alternatives). Warrant is graded by which alternatives have been eliminated, on four grades from "weak warrant" to "proof". Where to stop probing is set by context, meaning background knowledge, a default entitlement to other scientists' claims, and cost-benefit values. On this account there is no gold standard: RCTs are good only because they eliminate many alternatives at once. The 1950s smoking–lung cancer case illustrates the theory; it does not test it. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-197](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-197.md)); worth reading as the clearest short statement of an eliminativist, anti-hierarchy account of evidence that works for observational science, and of how values and trust set stopping rules. Its grades of warrant and pragmatic guidelines are proposals illustrated on one historical case, not validated criteria. |
| [LIT-119](../../record/literature.d/LIT-119.md) | Conspiracy Theories and Public Trust | Keeley (2023), DOI-10.5040/9781978734494.ch-011. Public Trust Skepticism — discounting conspiracy theories because they erode trust in institutions — gets things backwards: for an important class of theories it is the sweeping distrust they presuppose that undermines their own credibility, by leading to "epistemic nihilism". | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found no open copy: the chapter is in a closed-access Lexington Books volume, PhilPapers refused the request, and the author lists it on his CV without posting it. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-120](../../record/literature.d/LIT-120.md) v2 | Welfare Subjects and Autopoiesis | Bruckner (2025), DOI-10.1017/apa.2025.10006. Bruckner proposes, by abduction and not deduction (§4), that being autopoietic is sufficient, not necessary, for being a welfare subject. An entity is autopoietic when it "sustains, maintains, and renews itself in a way that responds and adapts to its environmental conditions". On this view insentient plants and a hypothetical non-sentient self-maintaining car ("Tessler") are welfare subjects, while engines with governors, thermostats and heat-seeking missiles are not. He answers the charge of absurd obligations in two ways: welfare value may not generate agent-neutral reasons at all, and if it does, the reasons concerning non-conscious subjects are "extremely weak" (Bradford 2023). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-093](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-093.md)); worth reading as the clearest short statement of a welfare criterion that is not based on sentience and admits machines. Read it knowing that its "autopoiesis" is a loose behavioural notion of adaptive self-maintenance, not Maturana and Varela's self-production, and that the line it draws against thermostats is argued as one of degree ("much richer", §6.1) while being stated as one of kind. |
| [LIT-121](../../record/literature.d/LIT-121.md) v2 | The Meaning of the Wave Function: In Search of the Ontology of Quantum Mechanics | Gao (2016), [ARXIV-1611.02738](https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.02738). Gao argues first that ψ is real. His case uses protective measurements, which yield ⟨A⟩ on a single system with certainty, in three versions (ch. 4): one in the ontological-models framework using preparation non-contextuality, two he says need no auxiliary assumption, and one that uses a new criterion of reality. He then argues that ψ describes random discontinuous motion (RDM) of N point particles in 3-space: \|ψ\|² is the density with which each particle is present at each instant, and ψ is a single-case propensity. The main support is that the charge density \|ψ\|²Q, which protective measurement shows to exist, has no self-interaction (chs. 6–7). If the Born probabilities come from RDM, collapse is real. Gao proposes an energy-conserving, Planck-time-discrete collapse model: Pᵢ(t+t_P) = Pᵢ(t) + (ΔE/E_P)[δ_{EsEi} − Pᵢ(t)], giving τ_c ≈ ħE_P/(ΔE)² (ch. 8). Combined with relativity, it requires a detectable preferred Lorentz frame (ch. 9). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-140); worth reading as the fullest single statement of the protective-measurement argument for ψ-reality and of the RDM ontology. Its chapters 2, 3, 7.1 and 8.5 are also a well-referenced critical survey of ψ-epistemic, nomological, configuration-space-realist, Penrose and CSL views. Its central premises are contested (see Limitations), its collapse model is unconfirmed, and chapters 8–9 are openly speculative. |
| [LIT-122](../../record/literature.d/LIT-122.md) v2 | The Representation of Time and Change in Mechanics | Belot (2005), DOI-10.1016/B978-044451560-5/50005-1. For theories with ideal existence, uniqueness and a dynamical time-translation group, time is three intertwined ℝ-actions (on spacetime, on the symplectic space of solutions, and on the isomorphic space of initial data), and change is a slicing-indexed family of functions on those spaces. Singular dynamics, gauge freedom and time-dependence each damage this picture in a repairable way. Spatially compact vacuum GR breaks it, because the reduced Hamiltonian vanishes (h ≡ 0) and there is only one canonical isomorphism between the reduced spaces. That is the problem of time. It is not an artefact of the 3+1 split, it does not show change to be illusory, and it is absent from asymptotically flat GR and from artificially generally covariant theories. A parameterized "geometric time", such as CMC, restores change and, in the CMC case, a time-dependent Hamiltonian flow. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-118](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-118.md)); worth reading as the careful technical-philosophical statement of what the problem of time is and is not in classical GR. It is long and assumes graduate differential geometry, and it offers a diagnosis, not a solution. Its quantum-gravity motivations date from 2005 and are not updated here. |
| [LIT-123](../../record/literature.d/LIT-123.md) v2 | Reality as a Vector in Hilbert Space | Carroll (2021), [ARXIV-2103.09780](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.09780). A programmatic essay, not a result paper. Carroll argues that a quantum theory is fixed, up to unitary equivalence, by the Hamiltonian's eigenvalue list {E_n} on a finite-dimensional factor (he assumes a non-degenerate spectrum), and that parts are not given. Subsystems (tensor factorisations H = H_S ⊗ H_E or ⨂_α H_α), classicality and space are to be selected afterwards as rare structure that suits the dynamics. The only uniqueness he reports is for local factorisations, and it rests on a cited theorem (Cotler et al. 2019). The system/environment criterion rests on "simple numerical examples" in a companion paper, the metric and Einstein's equation on "optimistic assumptions", and emergent fields on a hope. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-134); worth reading as the shortest authoritative statement of Hilbert-space fundamentalism and its "quantum mereology" programme, with the caution that nothing is derived in it. Every technical claim is delegated to a companion paper, and the author says the programme "may ultimately fail" (p. 12). |
| [LIT-124](../../record/literature.d/LIT-124.md) v2 | Mereology | Varzi (2003), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/mereology/>. The entry argues that no mereological principle, not even the partial-order core M (Reflexivity, Transitivity, Antisymmetry), is merely a fixing of the meaning of "part". It lays out the ladder M ⊂ MM (+Supplementation) ⊂ EM (+Strong Supplementation) ⊂ GEM (+Unrestricted Sum). GEM is "virtually" a complete Boolean algebra with zero removed, and it is reached from M by exactly three adequate pairings: Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum2, Strong Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum3, and Complementation + Unrestricted Sum1. The influential Simons (1987) pairing, Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum3, which the entry's own 2003 edition also used, does not yield GEM. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-163](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-163.md)); the standard, current (revised June 2026) and formally exact map of the field, and the right background reference for the record's `mereology` topic. Two caveats: by its note 1 it excludes the systems-theory and ecological senses of "mereology", and by note 18 it excludes quantum part–whole formalisms. |
| [LIT-125](../../record/literature.d/LIT-125.md) v2 | The moral psychology of obligation | Tomasello (2019), DOI-10.1017/S0140525X19001742. Tomasello proposes that the human sense of obligation is "the internalized social/rational pressure from a joint or collective self-regulating agent 'we'… to do what 'we' expect me to" (§3). It arises first, around age 3, inside joint-commitment collaboration with a partner, and then, from about 3–5 years, toward in-group members through collective identification. It is a "self-conscious motivation" whose coercive force is a threat to one's cooperative identity. The support is a synthesis of developmental and comparative experiments, mostly from his own lab, on WEIRD children versus chimpanzees; the paper runs no new experiment. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-185](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-185.md)); worth reading as the standard statement of the shared-intentionality account of obligation and, above all, as a treatment. The commentaries by Bratman, Darwall, Gilbert and Pettit, and the response, are where the naturalistic claim is actually contested. The target article alone is a review-cum-hypothesis, not a demonstration. |
| [LIT-126](../../record/literature.d/LIT-126.md) v2 | The Category Error in Contemporary AI Safety Discourse and Why Non-Sentient Systems Cannot Be Moral Machines | Vieira (2026), <https://philarchive.org/archive/VIETCE>. A ten-page position paper. Its one explicit argument is "P1: moral status requires phenomenal consciousness; P2: current and foreseeable AI lacks it; C: such AI lacks moral status" (§3, p. 3). P1 rests on Swanepoel 2020, and P2 is asserted without argument. From this Vieira recommends that AI-safety frameworks measure only effects on sentient beings (§6.4). His replies retreat from C to a weaker norm, "we should not attribute moral status absent positive evidence of phenomenal consciousness" (§6, p. 6; §7.1), and he never says what that evidence would be. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-128](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-128.md)); not worth a reader's time. Its load-bearing premise (P2) is asserted, not argued. The abstract promises replies from human–AI interaction studies and global perspectives that the body does not contain. The "moral zombie" framing and the sentientist criterion are taken from Véliz (2021) and Swanepoel (2020), neither held here, and a reader gets both more directly from those sources. |
| [LIT-127](../../record/literature.d/LIT-127.md) v2 | Hutchinson’s ecological niche for individuals | Takola & Schielzeth (2022), DOI-10.1007/s10539-022-09849-y. The paper defines the individualized (ecological) niche as the environmental conditions under which a particular individual has an expected lifetime reproductive success of ≥ 1, counting each offspring as 0.5 per parent in outcrossers. The authors split it into five sub-concepts: realized (A), potential (B), time-slice (C), prospective (D) and trajectory-based (E), plus a "fundamental individualized niche" set aside as practically interchangeable with the potential niche. The individual niche has n + s dimensions, adding intraspecific axes such as density and morph frequency. Its boundary is the expected-LRS isocline of 1, which is estimable only by marginalizing fitness functions across individuals. The authors themselves call the concept "mostly of a metaphorical value". | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-170](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-170.md)); worth reading as the clearest structuring of the "individualized niche" vocabulary, and as the individual-level complement to Trappes ([LIT-169](../../record/literature.d/LIT-169.md)); it is a behavioural-ecology position essay, and its own admission that the potential niche is unobservable and estimable only from population-level marginalization limits it to a framework rather than a measurement result. |
| [LIT-128](../../record/literature.d/LIT-128.md) | Neurodiversity & evaluation: a defense (or not) of affective fictionalism | Fernández Castro et al. (2026), DOI-10.1080/09515089.2026.2638974. Affective fictionalism — reading mental-state attributions as normative evaluations rather than factual reports — is argued to meet three demands the neurodiversity paradigm places on any theory of folk psychology. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found no reachable copy: Taylor & Francis refused the request, no repository copy or preprint was found, and the first author's site links only to the publisher. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-129](../../record/literature.d/LIT-129.md) v2 | Epistemic injustice in the clinical care of practitioners of Afro-Brazilian religions | Porcher & Santaella (2026), DOI-10.1080/09515089.2026.2623889. When clinicians override a patient's religious interpretation of her experience in domains where medicine has no interpretive authority ("interpretive exclusivism"), they commit an epistemic injustice — racialised in the Brazilian case — that the authors say only epistemic humility remedies. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found no reachable copy: Taylor & Francis refused the request, and no preprint or repository copy was found (the UFRGS repository did not respond). It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-130](../../record/literature.d/LIT-130.md) v2 | Personal Identity and Ethics | Dufner (2025), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/identity-ethics/>. A survey, arguing no thesis of its own, of how three contested questions bear on ethics: which account of persistence is true (unique psychological continuity, Shoemaker and Parfit; or biological continuity and animalism, Olson and DeGrazia), whether identity or the psychological relations (continuity and/or connectedness) is what matters (Parfit's fission case), and whether metaphysics constrains ethics or the reverse. It traces these through prudential concern, responsibility, abortion (Marquis), death and brain-death criteria, dementia and advance directives (Dworkin vs Jaworska on Margo), and the separateness of persons (Rawls, Parfit, Nozick, Taurek, Sidgwick, Brink). Its main recurring finding is that the relation that fixes persistence and the relation that matters ethically keep coming apart. It concludes that all three questions remain open. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-166](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-166.md)); worth reading as the current, compact reference map from theories of persistence to applied ethics; it is a survey that takes no side, so it is a route to the primary sources (Parfit 1984, Olson 1997, McMahan 2002, Schechtman 1996, Korsgaard 1989) rather than a source of claims. |
| [LIT-131](../../record/literature.d/LIT-131.md) v2 | A Model of Understanding in Deep Learning Systems | Freeborn (2026), [ARXIV-2604.04171](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04171).
