Argues that AI compromises human free will by shaping decisions, manipulating emotions and lacking responsiveness to reasons, while admitting the debate is unresolved. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and no full text could be obtained (closed access on IEEE Xplore; no copy found via OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, CORE, Europe PMC or the Wayback Machine). It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-198](../../record/literature.d/LIT-198.md) v2 | Rule Consequentialism | Hooker (2003), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/consequentialism-rule/>. A survey written by the view's leading defender. It argues that full rule-consequentialism is best formulated in expectablist terms (Sophisticated criterion: the rules with greatest expected good, ties broken by closeness to conventional morality), counting the costs of teaching and internalising a code in new generations and assuming incomplete acceptance (the ~90% "overwhelming majority" formulation or a "teaching formulation"). So formulated, it escapes the collapse/incoherence dilemma and the rule-worship charge (§8), while demandingness in other possible worlds, contingency of justification, the teachers problem and rule conflict remain open (§9). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-160](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-160.md)); worth reading as the standard, carefully argued reference on rule-consequentialism and its §8 replies, provided it is read as an advocate's survey (Hooker is the view's main contemporary proponent) rather than a neutral one. |
| [LIT-199](../../record/literature.d/LIT-199.md) v3 | The Philosophy and Physics of Duality | De Haro & Butterfield (2025), [ARXIV-2508.01616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01616). The book gives a formal Schema: a duality is an isomorphism between the "model roots" (state space, quantities, dynamics) of two models of a common-core bare theory, with values and dynamics matching. It pairs this with an interpretative condition: duals are physically equivalent iff (A) they are duals and (B) i1 = i2∘d. On that basis it argues four things. First, there are no genuine cases of in-principle underdetermination in the examples, and a cautious realist can commit to the common core. Second, dualities do not favour structural realism. Third, duality is formally incompatible with ontological emergence, except "beside" it (AdS/CFT's RG direction), via effective dualities, or epistemically. Fourth, quasi-dualities motivate a "geometric view" on which models are charts on a successor theory's moduli space. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-178); worth reading as the reference treatment of dualities in philosophy of physics. It supplies definitions (the Schema, §3.1, and physical equivalence, §12.1.2, p. 402) and a common vocabulary (internal/external interpretation, common core, quasi-duality) that the rest of the record's duality works either use or argue against. Its physics examples are admittedly partly conjectural (AdS/CFT, Montonen–Olive and M-theory are unproven), and its realism is a stated position, not a derivation. |
| [LIT-200](../../record/literature.d/LIT-200.md) v2 | Wave-functionalism | Allori (2021), DOI-10.1007/s11229-021-03332-z. Allori proposes that the wave function be defined by three roles — empirical, nomological and explanatory. On this view it is an ingredient that "partially realizes" the law, not a law and not a material field. So its time-dependence and non-uniqueness (a phase shift, a density matrix) are harmless, it counts as non-epistemic and ontic, and it can take part in constructive explanation. Each of these is argued informally, and the escape from PBR is by relabelling the ontic/epistemic distinction, not by engaging the theorem. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-154](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-154.md)); a distinct and clearly placed option in the ψ-as-law family ([LIT-204](../../record/literature.d/LIT-204.md) §4.2). It is unproven until "part of the law" and "non-representational yet ontic" are given content that separates the view from Humean nomologism, and until it answers the "too cheap" objection it invites. |
| [LIT-201](../../record/literature.d/LIT-201.md) | Alternative formulations of multilevel selection | Damuth & Heisler (1988), DOI-10.1007/BF00647962. Multilevel selection comes in two formulations — MLS1, the effects of group membership on individual fitness, and MLS2, groups' own tendencies to go extinct or found new groups — which answer different questions about one process, and much of the group-selection and species-selection dispute comes from running them together. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found the article paywalled at Springer, with no open, repository or author copy. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-202](../../record/literature.d/LIT-202.md) v2 | Consent and the Formation of Preferences | Pettigrew (2026), DOI-10.1111/phpr.70131. Whether consent based on a preference switches the moral facts depends on how the preference was formed, and the criterion is "the space and resources those mechanisms leave for the free exercise of agency" (p. 5, restated p. 9): preferences formed by unjust Sanctions, or by Beliefs formed in an unjustly manipulated evidential environment, diminish consent's effectiveness, while preferences formed by Attention do not, even when an unjust force imposed the contact. Effectiveness is graded, and because the relevant mechanism is usually opaque, uncertainty about whether received consent is effective is "ubiquitous and quotidian" (p. 10). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-144](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-144.md)); worth reading as a short, clearly organised proposal that turns the adaptive-preferences debate into a question about mechanisms and free agency; the criterion is argued by cases and left rough by the author's own account, and one of the seven mechanisms it promises to sort (Amor Fati) is never sorted. |
| [LIT-203](../../record/literature.d/LIT-203.md) | Conspiracy theorists are not the problem; Conspiracy liars are | Keeley (2024), DOI-10.1080/0020174X.2024.2375771. Many conspiracy theories start not from errant data but from lies and fabricated facts, so the pernicious cases are bad-faith pseudo-explanations — and science has analogous value-driven false starting points. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found no reachable copy: Taylor & Francis, PhilPapers and ResearchGate refused the request, and the author lists the paper on his CV without posting it. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-204](../../record/literature.d/LIT-204.md) v2 | Realism about the Wave Function | Chen (2018), [ARXIV-1810.07010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.07010). A survey that sorts realist readings of the non-relativistic universal wave function into four ontological readings (a field on 3N-dimensional space, a multi-field on 3-space, properties of systems as in spacetime state realism, a Hilbert-space vector), two nomological ones (strong: time-independent and simple like the classical Hamiltonian; weak: a Humean best-system variable) and Maudlin's sui generis option. It tabulates their compatibility with BM, GRW0/m/f and S0/Sm and with high- versus low-dimensional fundamental space (Table 1, 10 of 56 cells "?"), and declares no winner. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-175); worth reading as the compact map of the realist options and the hub for the wave-function cluster (LIT-200, LIT-100, LIT-164, LIT-121, LIT-176), with the caution that Table 1 is asserted, not derived, and is uneven in places. |
| [LIT-205](../../record/literature.d/LIT-205.md) v2 | One equation to rule them all: a philosophical analysis of the Price equation | Luque (2016), DOI-10.1007/s10539-016-9538-y. Luque argues that the Price equation, Δz̄ = Cov(w, z) + E(wΔz) (eq. 1, p. 7), is a Kuhnian "generalization-sketch", like f = ma. It is not a model, has no predictive power on its own, and becomes empirical only when a specific model interprets it. On that reading the critics' charges (van Veelen 2005; van Veelen et al. 2012; Nowak & Highfield 2011) that it is "a tautology" and "lacks predictive power" are either false or ask too much, and he recommends that evolutionary biologists adopt it as their unifying framework. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-101](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-101.md)); worth reading as a clear statement of the generalization-sketch reading of the Price equation and as a map of its extensions up to 2015 (Tables 2–3). The philosophical argument is thin in places, notably the Gödel-based case that it is "not a tautology", so it should be read as one side of the dispute and not as a settlement of it. |
| [LIT-206](../../record/literature.d/LIT-206.md) v3 | What Does ChatGPT Want? An Interpretationist Guide | Goldstein & Lederman (2025), DOI-10.1080/0020174X.2026.2727584. Taking as given an "objective" interpretationism, on which a system has beliefs and desires iff the hypothesis that it is rational and has them predicts its behaviour well enough on accuracy, power and tractability, the authors argue three things. The agent is the per-conversation instance, not the model. The case that instances have beliefs and desires is only "presumptive", resting on tractability. The desire hypothesis is "provisionally" HHH+0: helpful, honest and harmless, plus in-context "zero-shot" intrinsic desires. The decisive HHH vs HHH+0 comparison is a conjecture that the authors say needs further empirical work. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-138](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-138.md)); Worth reading as the clearest statement of interpretationist realism about LLM instance agents. It turns "does it want?" into comparisons of rival desire hypotheses against named behavioural results, and it is candid that its central empirical discriminations are untested. |
| [LIT-207](../../record/literature.d/LIT-207.md) v2 | Talkative AI and the fiction of artificial minds | Roberts (2026), DOI-10.1007/s11098-026-02560-8. If a substantial class of mental states (interoceptive, affective, perceptual, and bodily-agentive states such as effort, trying and ownership) depends on an animate body, then today's disembodied chatbots lack them, and their many self-ascriptions of such states are "systematically untrue". Roberts argues that users can nonetheless engage chatbots through make-believe, as a new kind of fictional character that is interactive, co-constructed, self-involving and blended with the real world. He argues that this fictionalist stance escapes the epistemic norms that bind realist belief. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-157](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-157.md)); worth reading as a short, clear statement of chatbot fictionalism. Its anti-realist premise is explicitly conditional and targets only body-dependent states, which leaves belief and reasoning open. |
| [LIT-208](../../record/literature.d/LIT-208.md) v2 | Distributional Semantics, Holism, and the Instability of Meaning | Grindrod et al. (2024), [ARXIV-2405.12084](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12084). The authors argue that distributional models answer the instability objection to meaning holism if meaning is read "differentially", as a word's relations to its nearest neighbours, and not as its position in the space. They illustrate this with two corpus-expansion demonstrations. In a ±10-word count model on Stein's The Making of Americans (about 522k tokens), adding Hemingway's "A Clean, Well-Lighted Place" (under 1,500 words) shifts every cosine value for "know" by at most about 0.0005 (e.g. 0.95231 → 0.95284 for "understand") and leaves its top-10 neighbours in the same order, while "glass" gets a new neighbourhood. A Word2vec CBOW model on 143M words of Wikipedia shows the same pattern when about 21M words of the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy (SEP) are added: "atomism" shifts, "can" barely moves. The residual instability, they argue, is a virtue. | Proposed — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-158); promising as a philosophical reply, but its empirical half rests on top-10 neighbour tables for four hand-picked words, with no stability metric and no seed replication. It would be settled by a systematic measurement of neighbour-set stability against word frequency across corpus increments and random seeds, which Antoniak & Mimno (2018) partly supply and the paper cites but does not reproduce. |
| [LIT-209](../../record/literature.d/LIT-209.md) v2 | The Phenomenology of Ritual Resistance: Colin Kaepernick as Confucian Sage | Walsh (2021), DOI-10.1007/s11712-020-09759-4. Walsh argues, drawing on Confucian texts (the Analects, Mengzi, Xunzi) and Merleau-Pontian phenomenology, that Colin Kaepernick's kneeling during the U.S. national anthem is "Confucian incivility" enacted bodily within the ritual. On his reading it rebukes the ritual's current object while staying reverent towards its ideal. He further argues it is "sagely" in a deliberately deflated sense: its "citational force" founds a widely imitated bodily expression of patriotism. The paper is interpretive; it reports no data and states no formal thesis. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-164](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-164.md)); worth reading as a careful worked example of how a norm-governed bodily practice can be contested from inside, and of Nivison and Hutton's diachronic reading of Xunzian sagehood put to use. Its standing is as comparative-philosophy interpretation, and its central normative notion (sagehood as uptake) is under-argued. |
| [LIT-210](../../record/literature.d/LIT-210.md) v2 | Philosophy of Microbiology | O'Malley & Parke (2020), <https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/microbiology/>. O'Malley & Parke argue in four steps that philosophy should attend to microbes. Microbes do most of the planet's biological work and underlie every major transition. Microbiology founded medicine, molecular biology and genomics. Microbes are unusually good material models, including for phenomena once defined by animals (predation, ageing, kin recognition, cognition). Microbiome research bears on individuality and causation. They are sceptical about the strongest microbiome claims. Whether host and microbiome form one unit of selection is "still disputed". Claims that microbiomes change the "self" are "usually expressed in very vague language". The 2026 revision adds that Asgard-archaea phylogenies may make eukaryogenesis "first and foremost a story about diversification within Archaea" rather than a transition in individuality. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-159](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-159.md)); worth reading as a short, current (April 2026) orientation to where microbes change philosophy of biology, with a useful deflationary check on microbiome-and-self claims. It is a survey with no thesis beyond "pay attention to microbes"; for individuality it points onward to the Biological Individuals ([LIT-168](../../record/literature.d/LIT-168.md)) and Units and Levels of Selection ([LIT-160](../../record/literature.d/LIT-160.md)) entries. |
| [LIT-211](../../record/literature.d/LIT-211.md) v3 | What Lives? A meta-analysis of diverse opinions on the definition of life | Bender et al. (2025), [ARXIV-2505.15849](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15849). Three LLMs (Claude 3.7 Sonnet, GPT-4o, Llama 3.3 70B), each run 3 times on both orderings of every pair, rated 68 hand-solicited definitions of life on a −1 to 1 "correlation" rubric. The averaged matrix, cut by complete-linkage clustering at an elbow, gives 8 clusters, 45/68 of them (66%) in "Cognitive Autonomy" (n=24) and "Dissipative Self-Organizing Systems" (n=21). The clusters are weak: the repo's own saved statistics give an overall silhouette of 0.079, and cross-model agreement is an unadjusted Rand index of 71–79% against a chance level of about 62–74%. The headline that life is "a continuous semantic landscape" is not tested against any alternative. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-109); worth reading as a dataset: 68 signed, three-sentence definitions of life from named researchers, with a manual thematic coding (Table 1). Its computational conclusions are weakly supported and are better read as illustrations than as findings. |
| [LIT-212](../../record/literature.d/LIT-212.md) v2 | Do Large Language Models Hallucinate Electric Fata Morganas? | Šekrst (2025), [ARXIV-2608.18816](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18816). A conceptual essay whose one substantive thesis is this. Following Dziri et al., hallucination includes any output "not rooted in the model's source data", including expressed emotions and opinions (§6). An LLM's apparent inner life is therefore always a candidate hallucination, so any real AI consciousness "might always remain epistemically inaccessible" (§8). The empirical support is two unquantified anecdotes: a Titanic question put to gpt-3.5-turbo and GPT-4 at unstated temperatures, and an extractive-QA BERT mislabelled "WikiBERT". Neither can bear the weight the paper puts on it. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-26 (NOTE-176); not worth a reader's time. Its one idea, that self-reports of inner states are exactly what a system trained on human text would produce whether or not it has them, is a single paragraph's point. Its experiments are anecdotes with no reported settings, one of them on a mislabelled model. It asserts both that current LLMs lack understanding and that machine consciousness would be undetectable, and never reconciles the two. |
| [LIT-213](../../record/literature.d/LIT-213.md) v2 | Rigour versus the need for evidential diversity | Cartwright (2021), DOI-10.1007/s11229-021-03368-1. Cartwright offers a characterisation of "rigour": a method is rigorous when a valid argument shows its results are evidence for the conclusion and its premises are well established or secured by design. On that definition even well-run RCTs often fail to rigorously establish a study-population ATE, because orthogonality holds only at assignment and the potential-outcomes premise carries heavy metaphysics, and they seldom answer singular questions. Her alternative is a causal-process-tracing theory of change (pToC) with five elements (steps, causal principles, support factors, derailers, safeguards). It catalogues the diverse and mostly non-rigorous evidence that singular causal predictions need. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-161](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-161.md)); worth reading as a compact, citable statement of the "rigour is overvalued, evidence the causal process" position. The pToC template is concrete, but the paper openly offers no method for weighing the evidence it catalogues. |
| [LIT-214](../../record/literature.d/LIT-214.md) | Blunting concepts: The double-edged effect of popularizing psychotherapy language | Almagro & Isern-Mas (2025), DOI-10.1080/09515089.2025.2573763. The spread of mental-health vocabulary into everyday talk comes from two distinct linguistic practices. "Therapy-speak", which reframes interpersonal or moral situations as psychological needs, blunts the concepts that people with mental health conditions need to describe their experience, and so produces hermeneutical injustice. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract on 2026-09-26; a close reading was attempted the same day and found no reachable copy: Taylor & Francis refused the request, the paper is closed access, and no preprint or repository copy was found. It stays Deferred until a copy is supplied. |
| [LIT-215](../../record/literature.d/LIT-215.md) v2 | Defending Informational Structural Realism: The View of Alternatives | Karpenko (2025), DOI-10.5840/eps202562230. Karpenko argues against V.E. Terekhovich's non-eliminative OSR, which holds that entities and structures exist but "objects do not exist". He extends Floridi's informational structural realism with one thesis: empirically equivalent but structurally different models (wave/particle/field descriptions, superstring dualities, AdS/CFT holography, equivalent programs in digital physics) have equal ontological standing. Since nothing stands "behind" them with a privileged configuration, the descriptions, i.e. information, are themselves fundamental, and theoretical objects exist as ontological primitives. There is no formal argument, and the physics is presented by assertion, with several errors. | Rejected — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-096](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-096.md)); not worth a reader's time on this topic. It misreports its source (Floridi, [LIT-152](../../record/literature.d/LIT-152.md)) on the points it builds on, and it states contested or wrong physics as established. Its one novel move, reading dualities as equal-status alternatives rather than underdetermination, is asserted, not argued, and is done properly in the duality literature ([LIT-113](../../record/literature.d/LIT-113.md), [LIT-199](../../record/literature.d/LIT-199.md)). |
| [LIT-216](../../record/literature.d/LIT-216.md) v2 | The Weirdness of the World | Schwitzgebel (2024), DOI-10.1515/9780691239309. The book's thesis is that every viable theory of how consciousness fits into the cosmos, and of the cosmos's basic structure, is both "bizarre" (contrary to common sense) and "dubious" (not rationally compelling). So "something that seems almost too crazy to believe must be true" (p. 4), and credence should be spread across the bizarre options. Examples of those options are that the United States is conscious (if materialism is true), that we are sims or Boltzmann brains (a 0.1–1% credence), that space is transcendentally ideal, that almost every act causes almost every future event, and that there is no fact about snail consciousness. Its only positive empirical results are three self-experiments against solipsism (for example, 17 of 19 decisive chess games lost, p < .001). Everything else is informal argument. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-188](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-188.md)); Worth reading as the one place Schwitzgebel's separately published arguments are joined into a single case: US consciousness, crazyism, 1% skepticism, Kant meets cyberpunk, the garden snail, and the Full Rights Dilemma for AI. Chapter 3, the Universal Bizarreness and Dubiety argument, is the load-bearing contribution. Its individual chapters are informal arguments, and the manuscript that can be reached is not the published text. The published book reorders the chapters, moves the objections appendix to the end and updates the AI chapter. |
| [LIT-217](../../record/literature.d/LIT-217.md) v3 | Science, Metaphysics and Structural Realism | Ladyman (2001), DOI-10.21825/philosophica.82261. Ladyman argues that structural realism should be metaphysically, not epistemically, revisionary. He gives three grounds. Quantum statistics leaves the individuality of particles underdetermined: permutation invariance gives 3 two-particle states, not 4, so probabilities are 1/3 rather than 1/4 (p. 62). Entangled states give non-supervenient relations that refute Humean supervenience (pp. 71–73). The hole argument blocks point-based substantivalism (pp. 68–69). He proposes that science describes "the modal structure of the phenomena" (p. 73), with modal realism supplying the no-miracles intuition. The paper ends non-eliminatively: "the phenomena have structure but they are not structure" (p. 74). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-192](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-192.md)); Worth reading as the earliest freely available statement of ontic structural realism by its originator. It argues the Humean-supervenience and quantum-individuality points more fully than the SEP survey ([LIT-045](../../record/literature.d/LIT-045.md)). It also shows that the 2001 view was a modest one, realism about modal structure with no eliminativism, before *Every Thing Must Go*. Its positive proposal takes two paragraphs (pp. 73–74) and is programmatic. |
| [LIT-218](../../record/literature.d/LIT-218.md) v2 | Ontic Structural Realism and Economics | Ross (2008), DOI-10.1086/594518. Ross argues that OSR, usually motivated only by fundamental physics, is also the right ontology for a mature special science, economics. The progress of economic theory from Jevons through Hicks–Allen (1934), Samuelson (1947), Debreu (1959) and Arrow (1965) consists in steadily removing psychological, individual-level hypotheses. Its basic objects are optimisation problems, above all games, which are "mathematical structures … whose relata are distinguished as such by the mathematical representations … just like quarks and bosons" (§3). So OSR, joined with an assumption that science is unified, gets consilient support from outside physics. Individuals, in physics and in economics, are "book-keeping devices". | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-189](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-189.md)); Worth reading as Ross's own short statement of what OSR says outside physics. It argues that individuals are bookkeeping heuristics while "tables and rocks" and people still exist, and that consilience across the sciences, not physics alone, can support OSR. Its history of economics is a sketch, and its central claims are asserted from the formalism rather than argued against a worked alternative. Cite the 2008 Philosophy of Science version. This note reads the 2006 preprint. |
| [LIT-219](../../record/literature.d/LIT-219.md) v2 | Patterns All the Way Up: Prolegomena to a Future Naturalized Metaphysics | Ladyman (2026), DOI-10.7551/mitpress/15550.003.0006. Ladyman restates the Ladyman–Ross metaphysics as fifteen theses drawn from scientific practice. The existence criterion is "to be is to be a real pattern", a pattern that "indispensibly figures in projectible generalisations" (p. 2). Realism is realism about natural modal structure, which is "absolute" in formal mode but "domain and scale-dependent" in material mode (thesis 4). There is no single hierarchy of levels and no assumed fundamental level (theses 10–11, fn. 9). Ontology is effective and scale-relative (thesis 12). Scientific real patterns are lossy compressions, unlike the Game of Life's; the ideal gas laws use 3 degrees of freedom for ~10²³ (thesis 14). He quotes ETMG's Principle of Naturalistic Closure verbatim and calls it "problematic" because it refers to fundamental physics (p. 8). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-191](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-191.md)); Worth reading as the most recent and most compact statement of rainforest realism by one of its two authors, and the one that explicitly revises *Every Thing Must Go*. It weakens the PNC, denies that "relational structure is fundamental" was meant as fundamentalism, and recasts real patterns modally and statistically as well as information-theoretically. Its fifteen theses are asserted and illustrated, not argued. It presupposes the book's definitions, which it does not restate. |
| [LIT-220](../../record/literature.d/LIT-220.md) v2 | Real Patterns | Dennett (1991), DOI-10.2307/2027085. A pattern is real in some data "if there is a description of the data that is more efficient than the bit map, whether or not anyone can concoct it" (p. 34). This is Chaitin's incompressibility definition of randomness turned around (p. 32). Dennett uses the criterion, with the Game of Life's gliders and eaters as the stepping stone (pp. 37–42), to defend "mild realism" about beliefs. Folk psychology tracks a real, noisy pattern of enormous predictive leverage. Two rival interpretations can be "different, but equally real, patterns" with no deeper fact to settle between them (p. 49). | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-190](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-190.md)); Worth reading as the source of the real-patterns criterion that rainforest realism generalises into a theory of existence, and as the paper whose exact definition the later literature modifies. It has a lossless-compression criterion, observer-relativity through "perspectives", and trade-offs between noise and simplicity. Its target is beliefs, not ontology in general. Dennett explicitly declines the label question ("I think that the view itself is clearer than either of the labels", p. 51). |
| [LIT-221](../../record/literature.d/LIT-221.md) v2 | Yoneda lemma | nLab (2009), <https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/Yoneda+lemma>. The page states that for a locally small category C, every presheaf X and every object c, there is a canonical bijection Hom_{[C^op,Set]}(y(c), X) ≅ X(c), given by η ↦ η_c(id_c). It proves the bijection by chasing id_c around a naturality square. From it the page derives that the Yoneda embedding y: c ↦ Hom_C(−,c) is fully faithful and that y(c) ≅ y(d) ⇔ c ≅ d. It also shows by counterexample that the hypothesis doing the work is naturality: objects whose hom-sets are pointwise in bijection need not be isomorphic. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-195](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-195.md)); Worth reading as a compact, correct statement and proof of the lemma with the embedding and the iso-determination corollary. It also has a "Necessity of naturality" section, which textbooks rarely include and which any structuralist reading of the lemma needs. Two caveats: its classical statement does not state naturality in c and X (the HoTT section does), and its finite counterexample is ill-formed as written. |
| [LIT-222](../../record/literature.d/LIT-222.md) v2 | No categorial support for radical ontic structural realism | Lam & Wüthrich (2015), DOI-10.1093/bjps/axt053. Lam and Wüthrich argue that category theory gives radical OSR no support. Identifying objects with identity arrows is relabelling (§3). Generalized elements X → A exist in every category, so elements are not eliminated (§4). Categories without products also lose set-style relations, "throwing out the relations with the relata" (§4). Morphisms relate C-objects, not the elements that stand for physical relata, and they "only distinguish isomorphism classes of structures" (§5). Bain's GR and TQFT cases eliminate at most spacetime points, not relata tout court (§§6–7). They conclude that category theory "offers little if any comfort to ROSR" (abstract) and favour "balanced" OSR. The Yoneda lemma is not named, although §§4–5 state two of its consequences. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-194](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-194.md)); Worth reading as the most careful direct refutation of Bain (yo2). Its main points (generalized elements, morphisms relate C-objects, determination only up to isomorphism, sections are not maximal ideals) are correct and are exactly what the Yoneda reading needs. It has a few category-theoretic slips of its own: Hilb said to lack products, a mis-definition of topos, and the claim that non-commutative algebras have no maximal ideals. |
| [LIT-223](../../record/literature.d/LIT-223.md) v2 | Category-theoretic structure and radical ontic structural realism | Bain (2013), DOI-10.1007/s11229-011-9896-6. Bain argues that the "relations without relata" objection to radical OSR depends on defining structure set-theoretically, where a relation is a subset of X × X. If a structure is instead "an object in a category", its elements (arrows 1 → A) are "not essential", because category-theoretic objects need not be structured sets. His examples are sheaves of Einstein algebras for GR with asymptotic boundary conditions, which may lack global sections (§3), and nCob and Hilb (§4). He concludes that ROSR "avoids the charge that it rests on an incoherent claim" (p. 1634). The paper never mentions the Yoneda lemma, representable functors or universal properties by name. | Active — read in full 2026-09-26 ([NOTE-193](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-193.md)); Worth reading as the short, canonical statement of the category-theoretic defence of radical OSR, which the later literature (yo3; Lal & Teh; Eva 2016) responds to. Read it with yo3. Its central inference is overstated, and its GR section contains technical errors that yo3 documents. |
| [LIT-224](../../record/literature.d/LIT-224.md) | Shannon Information and Kolmogorov Complexity | Grünwald & Vitányi (2004), [arXiv:cs/0410002](https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0410002). Shannon's and Kolmogorov's theories line up concept by concept (entropy vs complexity, probabilistic vs algorithmic mutual information and sufficient statistics, rate-distortion vs structure function), and in each pair the Shannon notion is, up to additive terms, the expectation of the Kolmogorov one. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-225](../../record/literature.d/LIT-225.md) | Minimum Description Length Induction, Bayesianism, and Kolmogorov Complexity | Vitányi & Li (1999), [arXiv:cs/9901014](https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/9901014). Ideal MDL, derived from Bayes's rule with the universal prior, coincides with Bayesian MAP selection exactly when the data are random with respect to the hypothesis and the hypothesis is random with respect to the prior (the "Fundamental Inequality"). For finite-set models it reduces to Kolmogorov's minimal sufficient statistic, so compression is almost always the best strategy for both identifying hypotheses and predicting. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-226](../../record/literature.d/LIT-226.md) | The Conditional Entropy Bottleneck | Fischer (2020), [ARXIV-2002.05379](https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.05379). Training a representation to minimize I(X;Z\|Y) while maximizing I(Y;Z) — the Conditional Entropy Bottleneck, targeting the "Minimum Necessary Information" point I(X;Y)=I(X;Z)=I(Y;Z) — empirically improves accuracy, adversarial robustness, OoD detection and calibration and refuses to memorize random labels. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-227](../../record/literature.d/LIT-227.md) | Contrastive Learning Is Spectral Clustering On Similarity Graph | Tan et al. (2023), [ARXIV-2303.15103](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.15103). SimCLR's unmodified InfoNCE loss is equivalent to spectral clustering on the augmentation-defined similarity graph (min tr(Zᵀ L(π) Z) + log R(Z)), CLIP does generalized spectral clustering on the image–text pair graph, and mixtures of exponential kernels (Kernel-InfoNCE) beat the Gaussian kernel. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-228](../../record/literature.d/LIT-228.md) | Contrastive and Non-Contrastive Self-Supervised Learning Recover Global and Local Spectral Embedding Methods | Balestriero & LeCun (2022), [ARXIV-2205.11508](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.11508). VICReg, SimCLR and Barlow Twins each solve, in closed form, a classical spectral embedding problem on the positive-pair graph G (Laplacian Eigenmaps, a generalized MDS/ISOMAP, and CCA respectively), so contrastive vs non-contrastive SSL is global vs local spectral embedding, and when G matches the downstream task any of them is optimal. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-229](../../record/literature.d/LIT-229.md) | Meaningful Information | Vitányi (2001), [arXiv:cs/0111053](https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0111053). With total recursive functions as the model class, the algorithmic minimal sufficient statistic ("sophistication") is a non-trivial measure of meaningful information. It matches the finite-set and probability-model versions up to additive terms, some objects are entirely meaningful with no residual randomness, and it is not computable. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |
| [LIT-230](../../record/literature.d/LIT-230.md) | Riesz representation theorem (Wikipedia) | Wikipedia contributors (2026), <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riesz_representation_theorem>. A Hilbert space is isometrically (anti-)isomorphic to its continuous dual — every continuous linear functional φ is x ↦ ⟨x, f_φ⟩ for a unique f_φ with ‖f_φ‖ = ‖φ‖ — and this identification is what defines adjoints and the bra–ket correspondence. | Deferred — seeded from the abstract and a skim on 2026-09-26; not read in full |

| [LIT-262](../../record/literature.d/LIT-262.md) v2 | The Geometry of Information Retrieval | van Rijsbergen (2004), DOI-10.1017/CBO9780511543333. The book recasts IR's vector-space, probabilistic and logical models in one Hilbert-space language. Documents are state vectors. Relevance and aboutness are Hermitian observables that need not commute. Subspaces carry a non-distributive logic whose conditional is shown to be a Stalnaker conditional equal to the Sasaki hook (ch. 5). Gleason's theorem makes tr(ρP) the probability of a subspace, which ch. 6 uses to rewrite cosine matching, cluster representatives, relevance and pseudo-relevance feedback, dynamic clustering and ostensive retrieval. It is a proposed language, not a tested model: no experiment is reported, and its "content hypothesis" that the probability of a concept is cos²θ is argued, not tested. | Active — read in full 2026-09-27 ([NOTE-239](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-239.md)); worth reading as the founding statement of the Hilbert-space (quantum-probability) programme in IR. It is short, self-contained and mathematically careful at the level it works at, and its ch. 5 proof that the subspace conditional is a Stalnaker conditional and its ch. 6 trace-formula recastings of standard IR operations are the parts to take away. Read it as a language, not as evidence: it reports no experiment, and every IR payoff it names is a reformulation or a research direction. |
